\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Bigg(\Bigg\{\frac{k_{j}k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}}\mathcal{F}\left(\partial_{x_{\ell}}\partial_{x_{i}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\varepsilon}(t,x,\xi)v(\xi)_{i}v(\xi)_{j}d\xi\right)\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\Bigg\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\Bigg)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}\partial_{x_{i}}\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)v(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}(\tau))_{i}v(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}(\tau))_{j}\mu(d\Theta)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\left\lVert\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}v(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon})_{i}v(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon})_{j}\mu(d\Theta)\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{\tau}{\eta}\right)^{-(\beta+1)}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\eta\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{t}{\eta}\right)^{-\beta}\sup_{\Theta}\left(\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}\right),

(3.33)

where we used (
2.3
) to handle the two derivatives, the algebra property (
2.1
), the relativistic bound in (
2.5
), and (
3.7
) for the integral in time.
Thus, multiplying (
3.33
) by
(\delta_{0}-\delta-t/\eta)^{\beta}
and summing it to (
3.32
), we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert I_{1}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq C\eta\sup_{\Theta}\left(\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}\right).

(3.34)

Estimate on
I_{2}
:
We now estimate
I_{2}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\big(\big\{\widehat{I_{2}}(k)\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\big)
. As before we use (
3.31
), therefore having

\displaystyle\left\lvert I_{2}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Bigg(\Bigg\{\frac{k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}}k\cdot\mathcal{F}\left(\varepsilon^{2}\big(E^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\big)\right)\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\Bigg\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\Bigg)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert\varepsilon^{2}E^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau.

Then using the algebra property (
2.1
), inequality (
2.2
) to handle the divergence operator, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert I_{2}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\left(\left\lvert\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}\left\lvert\varepsilon\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}+\left\lvert\varepsilon E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}\left\lvert\varepsilon\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}\right)d\tau

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{\tau}{\eta}\right)^{-\beta}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\eta\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}},

(3.35)

where we used (
3.4
) to estimate the integral in time.

Next, we compute
\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}I_{2}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}
for
\ell\in\{1,2,3\}
. As before we use (
3.31
), therefore, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}I_{2}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Bigg(\Bigg\{\frac{k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}}k\cdot\mathcal{F}\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{x_{\ell}}\big(E^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\big)\right)\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\Bigg\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\Bigg)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{x_{\ell}}\left(E^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau.

By the algebra property (
2.1
) and inequalities (
2.2
) and (
2.3
) to handle the derivatives we get

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}I_{2}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert\varepsilon\partial_{x_{\ell}}E^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}\left\lvert\varepsilon\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau

(3.36)

\displaystyle+2\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}\left\lvert\varepsilon\partial_{x_{\ell}}\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\int_{0}^{t}\left[\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{\tau}{\eta}\right)^{-2\beta}+\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{\tau}{\eta}\right)^{-(\beta+1)}\right]d\tau.

Multiplying (
3.36
) by
\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{t}{\eta}\right)^{\beta}
and using that
\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{t}{\eta}\right)^{\beta}\leq\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{\tau}{\eta}\right)^{\beta}
for
\tau\leq t
, we get

\displaystyle\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{t}{\eta}\right)^{\beta}\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}I_{2}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\int_{0}^{t}\Bigg[\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{\tau}{\eta}\right)^{-\beta}+\frac{\big(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{t}{\eta}\big)^{\beta}}{\big(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{\tau}{\eta}\big)^{\beta+1}}\Bigg]d\tau.

Using the values of the time integral (
3.4
) and (
3.7
) we conclude

\displaystyle\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{t}{\eta}\right)^{\beta}\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}I_{2}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}.

(3.37)

Thus, summing the two bounds in (
3.35
) and (
3.37
), we get

\displaystyle\left\lVert I_{2}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq C\eta\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}.

(3.38)

Estimate on
I_{3}
:
We now estimate
I_{3}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\big(\big\{\widehat{I_{3}}(k)\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\big)
. As before, using (
3.31
), we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert I_{3}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Bigg(\Bigg\{\frac{k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}}k\cdot\mathcal{F}\left(j^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\wedge B^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right)\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\Bigg\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\Bigg)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert(j^{\varepsilon}\wedge B^{\varepsilon})\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau.

Then, by the algebra property (
2.1
),

\displaystyle\left\lvert I_{3}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert j^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}\left\lvert B^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau\leq C\eta\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}},

(3.39)

where we used that
t\leq\eta
for the last inequality.

Next, we compute
\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}I_{3}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}
for
\ell\in\{1,2,3\}
. As before, using (
3.31
), we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}I_{3}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Bigg(\Bigg\{\frac{k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}}k\cdot\mathcal{F}\left(\partial_{x_{\ell}}(j^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\wedge B^{\varepsilon}(\tau))\right)\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\Bigg\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\Bigg)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}(j^{\varepsilon}\wedge B^{\varepsilon})(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{\tau}{\eta}\right)^{-\beta}\left\lvert j^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}\left\lvert B^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau,

where we used the algebra property (
2.1
) and inequality (
2.2
) to handle the derivative.
Finally, using that
\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{\tau}{\eta}\right)^{-\beta}\leq\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{t}{\eta}\right)^{-\beta}
for
\tau\leq t
, we conclude

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}I_{3}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{t}{\eta}\right)^{-\beta}\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\int_{0}^{t}ds\leq C\eta\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{t}{\eta}\right)^{-\beta}\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}},

(3.40)

where we used
t\leq\eta
for the last inequality.
Thus, multiplying (
3.40
) by
(\delta_{0}-\delta-t/\eta)^{\beta}
and summing it to (
3.39
), we get

\displaystyle\left\lVert I_{3}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq C\eta\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}.

(3.41)

Estimate on
I_{4}
:
Finally, we bound
I_{4}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\big(\big\{\widehat{I_{4}}(k)\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\big)
. As before we use (
3.31
), then we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert I_{4}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Bigg(\Bigg\{\frac{k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}}k\cdot\mathcal{F}\left(R^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right)\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\Bigg\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\Bigg)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert R^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau.

(3.42)

Recalling expression (
3.15
) for the remainder and formula (
1.11
) for
f^{\varepsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert I_{4}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x,\xi)\Bigl(\lambda(\xi)(E^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x)+v(\xi)\wedge B^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x))\Bigr)d\xi\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\Bigl(\lambda(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon})(E^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x)+v(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon})\wedge B^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x))\Bigr)\mu(d\Theta)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau,

where
\lambda(\xi):=\nabla_{\xi}\left(v(\xi)-\xi\right)
.
By the algebra property (
2.1
) and using
t\leq\eta
to bound the time integral, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert I_{4}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}\leq 2\eta\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\left[\left(\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\varepsilon\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert v(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon})\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right)\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\varepsilon^{-1}\lambda(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon})\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right].

Using inequalities (
2.5
) and (
2.6
) for the relativistic corrections
v(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})
and
\lambda(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})
, we get

\displaystyle|I_{4}(t)|_{\delta}\leq\varepsilon C\eta\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left(\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{3}\right).

(3.43)

Next, we compute
\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}I_{4}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}
for
\ell\in\{1,2,3\}
. Proceeding as for (
3.42
) and using inequality (
2.2
) to handle the
\partial_{x_{\ell}}
derivative, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}I_{4}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}R^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{\tau}{\eta}\right)^{-\beta}\left\lvert R^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau.

Next, using that
\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{\tau}{\eta}\right)^{-\beta}\leq\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{t}{\eta}\right)^{-\beta}
for
\tau\leq t
, we conclude

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}I_{4}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{t}{\eta}\right)^{-\beta}\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert R^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon C\eta\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{t}{\eta}\right)^{-\beta}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left(\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{3}\right),

(3.44)

where we used the same estimates as in (
3.43
) to bound
\left\lvert R^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}
.
Multiplying (
3.44
) by
(\delta_{0}-\delta-t/\eta)^{\beta}

and summing it to (
3.43
), we get

\displaystyle\left\lVert I_{4}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq\varepsilon C\eta\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left(\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{3}\right).

(3.45)

Final steps of the estimate on
G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}}
: Finally, combining estimates (
3.34
), (
3.38
), (
3.41
), and (
3.45
), there exists a constant
C
depending on
\delta_{0}
and
\beta
such that

\displaystyle\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta\left(\sup_{\Theta}\left(\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}\right)+\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon C\eta\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left(\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{3}\right)+\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},0}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}.

(3.46)

Estimates on
\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}
:

Recalling (
3.16
), we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon\widehat{E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}}(t,k)=-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}}\sin\left(\frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}\right)\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)ds+\varepsilon\widehat{E_{\text{irr},0}^{\varepsilon}}(t,k).

We observe that the formula for
\varepsilon\widehat{E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}}(t,k)
has the same structure as the one for
\widehat{G_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}}(t,k)
(
3.30
), the only difference is that

(1-\cos\left(\frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}\right))
in (
3.30
)
is now replaced by
\sin\left(\frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}\right)
. Therefore, using

\displaystyle\left\lvert\frac{k_{j}k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}}\sin\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\varepsilon}\right)\right\rvert\leq 1,

we can estimate
\varepsilon\widehat{E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}}
in the same way as
\widehat{G_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}}
and we get the same inequality as in (
3.46
). Namely, there exists a constant
C
depending on
\delta_{0}
and
\beta
such that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta\left(\sup_{\Theta}\left(\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}\right)+\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon C\eta\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left(\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{3}\right)+\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},0}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}.

(3.47)

3.2.2
Estimates on
G_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon},\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}}
and
B^{\varepsilon}

Estimates on
G_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}
:
Using formula (
3.21
), we have

\displaystyle\widehat{G_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}}(t,k)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{\mathrm{i}k\wedge\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(\tau,k)}{\varepsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}\sin\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}(s-\tau)}{\varepsilon}\right)d\tau ds+\widehat{G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},0}}(t,k),

where

\displaystyle\widehat{G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},0}}(t,k)=\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},0}}(s,k)ds

(3.48)

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}\widehat{E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}}(0,k)\sin\left(\frac{t\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{1+|k|^{2}}\widehat{\partial_{t}E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}}(0,k)\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{t\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right).

Using Fubini on the double integral and recalling that
\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}d\tau ds=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\tau}^{t}dsd\tau
, we get

\displaystyle\widehat{G_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}}(t,k)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mathrm{i}k\wedge\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(\tau,k)}{\varepsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}\int_{\tau}^{t}\sin\left(\frac{(s-\tau)\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)dsd\tau+\widehat{G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},0}}(t,k)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mathrm{i}k\wedge\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(\tau,k)}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}(1+|k|^{2})}\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{(t-\tau)\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)d\tau+\widehat{G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},0}}(t,k)

\displaystyle=:\widehat{J_{1}}(t,k)+\widehat{J_{2}}(t,k)+\widehat{J_{3}}(t,k)+\widehat{J_{4}}(t,k)+\widehat{G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},0}}(t,k),

(3.49)

where, by recalling the formula (
3.20
) for
h^{\varepsilon}
,
\{\widehat{J_{\ell}}(t,k)\}_{\ell=1}^{4}
are given by

\displaystyle\widehat{J_{1}}(t,k):=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}(1+|k|^{2})}\wedge\mathcal{F}\left(\nabla_{x}\wedge\partial_{x_{i}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\varepsilon}(\tau)v(\xi)_{i}v(\xi)d\xi\right)(\tau,k)\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{(t-\tau)\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)d\tau,

\displaystyle\widehat{J_{2}}(t,k):=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}(1+|k|^{2})}\wedge\mathcal{F}\left(\varepsilon^{2}\nabla_{x}\wedge\big(E^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x)\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x)\big)\right)(\tau,k)\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{(t-\tau)\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)d\tau,

\displaystyle\widehat{J_{3}}(t,k):=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}(1+|k|^{2})}\wedge\mathcal{F}\left(\nabla_{x}\wedge(j^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x)\wedge B^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x))\right)(\tau,k)\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{(t-\tau)\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)d\tau,

and

\displaystyle\widehat{J_{4}}(t,k):=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}(1+|k|^{2})}\wedge\mathcal{F}\left(\nabla_{x}\wedge R^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x)\right)(\tau,k)\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{(t-\tau)\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)d\tau.

We now derive the a priori estimates for the quantities
J_{\ell}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\big(\big\{\widehat{J_{\ell}}(k)\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\big)
with
\ell\in\{1,2,3,4\}
.

Estimate on
J_{1}
:
We start by estimating
J_{1}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\big(\big\{\widehat{J_{1}}(k)\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\big)
. By using that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}(1+|k|^{2})}\wedge\left(k\wedge\mathcal{F}(\cdot)\right)\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{(t-\tau)\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\right\rvert\leq 2\left\lvert\mathcal{F}(\cdot)\right\rvert,

(3.50)

and expression (
1.11
) for
f^{\varepsilon}(t,x,\xi)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{1}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\Bigg\{\frac{k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}(1+|k|^{2})}\wedge\left(k\wedge\mathcal{F}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\varepsilon}(t,x,\xi)v(\xi)_{i}v(\xi)d\xi\right)(\tau,k)\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left.\left.\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{(t-\tau)\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\Bigg\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\right)\right|_{\delta}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert\partial_{x_{i}}\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)v(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}(\tau))_{i}v(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}(\tau))\mu(d\Theta)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta\sup_{\Theta}\left(\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}\right),

(3.51)

where the last inequality is deduced using the same arguments as for (
3.32
).
Next, we estimate
\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}J_{1}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}
for
\ell\in\{1,2,3\}
. We use the bound (
3.50
) and expression (
1.11
) for
f^{\varepsilon}(t,x,\xi)
to get

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}J_{1}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\Bigg\{\frac{k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}(1+|k|^{2})}\wedge k\wedge\mathcal{F}\left(\partial_{x_{\ell}}\partial_{x_{i}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\varepsilon}(t,x,\xi)v(\xi)_{i}v(\xi)d\xi\right)(\tau,k)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left.\left.\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{(t-\tau)\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\Bigg\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\right)\right|_{\delta}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}\partial_{x_{i}}\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)v(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}(\tau))_{i}v(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}(\tau))\mu(d\Theta)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\eta\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{t}{\eta}\right)^{-\beta}\sup_{\Theta}\left(\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}\right),

(3.52)

where the last inequality is deduced using the same arguments as for (
3.33
).
Thus, multiplying (
3.52
) by
\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{t}{\eta}\right)^{\beta}
and summing it to (
3.51
), we get

\displaystyle\left\lVert J_{1}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq C\eta\sup_{\Theta}\left(\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}\right).

(3.53)

Estimate on
J_{2}
:
We now estimate
J_{2}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\big(\big\{\widehat{J_{2}}(k)\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\big)
. As before we use (
3.50
), therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{2}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\Bigg\{\frac{k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}(1+|k|^{2})}\wedge\left(k\wedge\mathcal{F}\left(\varepsilon^{2}\big(E^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x)\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x)\big)\right)(\tau,k)\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left.\left.\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{(t-\tau)\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\Bigg\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\right)\right|_{\delta}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert\varepsilon^{2}E^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\eta\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}},

(3.54)

where we used the same estimates as for (
3.35
) for the last inequality.

Next, we compute
\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}J_{2}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}
for
\ell\in\{1,2,3\}
. As before we use (
3.50
), therefore, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}J_{2}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\Bigg\{\frac{k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}(1+|k|^{2})}\wedge\left(k\wedge\mathcal{F}\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{x_{\ell}}\big(E^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x)\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x)\big)\right)(\tau,k)\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left.\left.\Bigg(1-\cos\Bigg(\frac{(t-\tau)\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\Bigg)\Bigg)\Bigg\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\right)\right|_{\delta}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{x_{\ell}}\left(E^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau.

Next, using the same argument as obtaining (
3.37
), we conclude

\displaystyle\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{t}{\eta}\right)^{\beta}\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}J_{2}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}.

(3.55)

Thus, summing inequalities (
3.54
) and (
3.55
) we get

\displaystyle\left\lVert J_{2}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq C\eta\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}.

(3.56)

Estimate on
J_{3}
:
We estimate
J_{3}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\big(\big\{\widehat{J_{3}}(k)\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\big)
. As before we use (
3.50
), therefore, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{3}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\Bigg\{\frac{k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}(1+|k|^{2})}\wedge\left(k\wedge\mathcal{F}\left((j^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x)\wedge B^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x))\right)(\tau,k)\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left.\left.\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{(t-\tau)\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\Bigg\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\right)\right|_{\delta}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert(j^{\varepsilon}\wedge B^{\varepsilon})\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau\leq C\eta\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}},

(3.57)

where we used the same inequalities as for (
3.39
).

Next, we compute
\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}J_{3}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}
for
\ell\in\{1,2,3\}
. We use again (
3.50
), therefore, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}J_{3}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\Bigg\{\frac{k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}(1+|k|^{2})}\wedge\left(k\wedge\mathcal{F}\left(\partial_{x_{\ell}}(j^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x)\wedge B^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x))\right)(\tau,k)\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left.\left.\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{(t-\tau)\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\Bigg\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\right)\right|_{\delta}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}(j^{\varepsilon}\wedge B^{\varepsilon})(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau\leq C\eta\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{t}{\eta}\right)^{-\beta}\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}},

(3.58)

where we used the same reasoning as for (
3.40
) to deduce the last inequality.
Thus, multiplying (
3.58
) by
\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{t}{\eta}\right)^{\beta}
and putting it together with (
3.57
), we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert J_{3}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq C\eta\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}.

(3.59)

Estimate on
J_{4}
:
We conclude by estimating
J_{4}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\big(\big\{\widehat{J_{4}}(k)\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\big)
. As before, using (
3.50
),

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{4}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\Bigg\{\frac{k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}(1+|k|^{2})}\wedge\left(k\wedge\mathcal{F}\left(\partial_{x_{\ell}}R^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x)\right)(\tau,k)\right)\right.\right.

(3.60)

\displaystyle\times\left.\left.\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{(t-\tau)\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\Bigg\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\right)\right|_{\delta}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert R^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau\leq\varepsilon C\eta\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left(\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{3}\right),

where we used the same estimates as in (
3.43
) for the last inequality.

Next, we compute
\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}J_{4}\right\rvert_{\delta}
for
\ell\in\{1,2,3\}
. We use (
3.50
) again and proceeding as for (
3.44
) we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}J_{4}(t)\right\rvert_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\Bigg\{\frac{k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}(1+|k|^{2})}\wedge\left(k\wedge\mathcal{F}\left(\partial_{x_{\ell}}\partial_{x_{\ell}}R^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x)\right)(\tau,k)\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left.\left.\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{(t-\tau)\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\Bigg\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\right)\right|_{\delta}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\left\lvert\partial_{x_{\ell}}R^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\rvert_{\delta}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon C\eta\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{t}{\eta}\right)^{-\beta}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left(\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{3}\right).

(3.61)

Thus, multiplying (
3.61
) by
\left(\delta_{0}-\delta-\frac{t}{\eta}\right)^{\beta}
and summing it with (
3.60
), we get

\displaystyle\left\lVert J_{4}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq\varepsilon C\eta\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left(\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{3}\right).

(3.62)

Final steps of the estimate on
G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}}
:
Finally, combining estimates (
3.53
), (
3.56
), (
3.59
), and (
3.62
), there exists a constant
C
depending on
\delta_{0}
and
\beta
such that

\displaystyle\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta\left(\sup_{\Theta}\left(\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}\right)+\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon C\eta\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left(\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{3}\right)+\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},0}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}.

(3.63)

Estimates on
\varepsilon E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}
:
Recalling (
3.21
), we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon\widehat{E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}}(t,k)=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{|k|^{2}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}\sin\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}(t-s)}{\varepsilon}\right)\left(\mathrm{i}k\wedge\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)\right)ds+\varepsilon\widehat{E_{\text{sol},0}^{\varepsilon}}(t,k).

We observe that
\varepsilon\widehat{E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}}(t,k)
is given by a similar formula that the one for
\widehat{G_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}}(t,k)
, i.e., (
3.49
). The only difference is that now we have
\sin\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}(t-s)}{\varepsilon}\right)
instead of
\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}(t-s)}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)
in (
3.49
). Therefore, using

\displaystyle\left\lvert\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}(1+|k|^{2})}\wedge\left(k\wedge\mathcal{F}(\cdot)\right)\sin\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}(t-s)}{\varepsilon}\right)\right\rvert\leq\left\lvert\mathcal{F}(\cdot)\right\rvert,

we can estimate
\varepsilon E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}
in the same way as
G_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}
and we get the same inequality as in (
3.63
). Namely, there exists a constant
C
depending on
\delta_{0}
and
\beta
such that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta\left(\sup_{\Theta}\left(\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}\right)+\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\varepsilon\eta\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left(\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{3}\right)+\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},0}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}.

(3.64)

Estimates on
B^{\varepsilon}
:
Recall that by the Maxwell–Faraday equation, we have

B^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=B^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x)-\int_{0}^{t}\nabla_{x}\wedge E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}}(s,x)ds.

From the formula for
\nabla_{x}\wedge E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}}
in (
3.19
), we get

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 20.06668pt\hskip-20.06668pt\hbox{$\nabla_{x}\wedge E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}$}\hskip-20.06668pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.13344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 20.06668pt}(t,k)=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{\varepsilon\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}\sin\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}(t-s)}{\varepsilon}\right)\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)ds+\hbox{\hskip 23.1917pt\hskip-23.1917pt\hbox{$\nabla_{x}\wedge E_{\text{sol},0}^{\varepsilon}$}\hskip-23.1917pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.13344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 23.1917pt}(t,k).

Therefore, by Fubini

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\hbox{\hskip 18.89369pt\hskip-18.89369pt\hbox{$\nabla_{x}\wedge E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}}$}\hskip-18.89369pt\hskip-3.375pt\raisebox{9.63577pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-3.375pt\hskip 18.89369pt}(s,k)ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{1}{\varepsilon\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}\sin\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}(s-\tau)}{\varepsilon}\right)\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(\tau,k)d\tau ds+\int_{0}^{t}\hbox{\hskip 21.92697pt\hskip-21.92697pt\hbox{$\nabla_{x}\wedge E_{\text{sol},0}^{\varepsilon}$}\hskip-21.92697pt\hskip-3.375pt\raisebox{9.63577pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-3.375pt\hskip 21.92697pt}(s,k)ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{1+|k|^{2}}\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}(t-\tau)}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(\tau,k)d\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\hbox{\hskip 21.92697pt\hskip-21.92697pt\hbox{$\nabla_{x}\wedge E_{\text{sol},0}^{\varepsilon}$}\hskip-21.92697pt\hskip-3.375pt\raisebox{9.63577pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-3.375pt\hskip 21.92697pt}(s,k)ds.

We note again that the last expression has a similar structure as
\widehat{G_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}}(t,k)
, i.e., (
3.49
). Therefore using

\displaystyle\left\lvert\frac{1}{1+|k|^{2}}\left(k\wedge\mathcal{F}(\cdot)\right)\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{(t-\tau)\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\right\rvert\leq 2\left\lvert\mathcal{F}(\cdot)\right\rvert,

we can bound
B^{\varepsilon}
with the same estimates as for
G_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}
. Thus, by (
3.63
), we obtain that there exists a constant
C
depending on
\delta_{0}
and
\beta
such that

\displaystyle\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}_{0}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert\int_{0}^{t}\nabla_{x}\wedge E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}}(s)ds\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}_{0}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\eta\left(\sup_{\Theta}\left(\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}\right)+\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\varepsilon\eta\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left(\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{3}\right)+\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},0}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}.

(3.65)

3.2.3
Estimates on
G_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon},\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}

Estimates on
G_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}
:
Using formula (
3.26
), we have

\displaystyle G_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\sin\left(\frac{s-\tau}{\varepsilon}\right)q^{\varepsilon}(\tau)d\tau ds+G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},0}(t),

where

\displaystyle G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},0}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},0}(s)ds=\varepsilon E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}(0)\sin\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)+\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}(0)\left(1-\cos\Bigl(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\Bigr)\right).

(3.66)

Then, by Fubini on the double integral and recalling that
\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}d\tau ds=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\tau}^{t}dsd\tau
, we get

\displaystyle G_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{t}q^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\int_{\tau}^{t}\sin\left(\frac{s-\tau}{\varepsilon}\right)dsd\tau+G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},0}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}q^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)d\tau+G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},0}(t)

\displaystyle=:K_{1}(t)+K_{2}(t)+K_{3}(t)+G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},0}(t).

(3.67)

By the expression (
3.25
) for
q^{\varepsilon}
,
\{K_{\ell}(t)\}_{\ell=1}^{3}
are given by

\displaystyle K_{1}(t):=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{0}^{t}\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}\varepsilon^{2}E^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x)\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x)dxd\tau,

\displaystyle K_{2}(t):=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{0}^{t}\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}j^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x)\wedge B^{\varepsilon}(\tau,x)dxd\tau,

and

\displaystyle K_{3}(t):=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{0}^{t}\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\widehat{R^{\varepsilon}}(\tau,0)d\tau.

In the following, being
G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}(t)
spatially homogeneous, we only need to compute
\left\lVert G_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}}
.

By using the bound

\displaystyle\left\lvert\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\right\rvert\leq 2,

and the fact that, for a general analytic function
\varphi:[0,\eta]\times\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}\varphi(t,x)dx\right\rvert=\left\lvert\widehat{\varphi}(t,0)\right\rvert\leq\left\lvert\varphi(t)\right\rvert_{\delta_{0}}\leq\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}},

we get

\left\lVert K_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}}\leq C\eta\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}},\qquad\left\lVert K_{2}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}}\leq C\eta\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

(3.68)

and

\left\lVert K_{3}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}}\leq C\varepsilon\eta\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left(\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{3}\right),

(3.69)

where we used the same estimates as in (
3.43
) for the last inequality.

Combining estimates (
3.68
), (
3.69
), there exists a constant
C
depending on
\delta_{0}
and
\beta
such that

\displaystyle\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta\left(\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\varepsilon\eta\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left(\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{3}\right)+\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},0}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}}.

(3.70)

Estimates on
\varepsilon E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}
:
Recalling (
3.24
), we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\sin\left(\frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}\right)q^{\varepsilon}(s)ds+\varepsilon E_{\text{mean},0}^{\varepsilon}(t).

We observe that the formula for
\varepsilon E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}(t)
has the same structure as the one for
G_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}(t)
(
3.67
), the only difference is that

(1-\cos\left(\frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}\right))
in (
3.67
)
is now replaced by
\sin\left(\frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}\right)
. Therefore, using

\displaystyle\left\lvert\sin\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\varepsilon}\right)\right\rvert\leq 1,

we can estimate
\varepsilon E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}
in the same way as
G_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}
and we get the same inequality as in (
3.70
). Namely, there exists a constant
C
depending on
\delta_{0}
and
\beta
such that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta\left(\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon C\eta\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left(\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{3}\right)+\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},0}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}}.

(3.71)

We now have all the a priori estimates, independent of
\varepsilon
, for the relevant quantities:
\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta},w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta},G^{\varepsilon},\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}
, and
B^{\varepsilon}
. In the next section, these estimates will allow us to construct an iterative scheme for these quantities. We will then use the a priori estimates within this scheme to demonstrate that its solutions converge to a solution of the Euler–Maxwell system (
1.12
).

3.3
Iterative scheme

We now build an iterative scheme to construct the analytic solutions declared in the statement of Theorem
1.1
.
For
t\in[0,\eta]
, let

\rho^{\varepsilon,(0)}_{\Theta}(t,x):=\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(0,x),\quad w^{\varepsilon,(0)}_{\Theta}(t,x):=\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(0,x)-G^{\varepsilon,(0)}(t,x),

where
\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(0)
and
\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(0)
are given by the hypothesis of Theorem
1.1
, while

G^{\varepsilon,(0)}(t,x):=G^{\varepsilon}_{0}(t,x)=G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},0}(t,x)+G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},0}(t,x)+G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},0}(t),

with
G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},0}(t,x)
,
G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},0}(t,x)
and
G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},0}(t)
defined in (
3.29
), (
3.48
) and (
3.66
).
Then, letting

E^{\varepsilon,(0)}(t,x):=\partial_{t}G^{\varepsilon,(0)}(t,x),\quad B^{\varepsilon,(0)}(t,x):=B^{\varepsilon}(0,x)-\nabla_{x}\wedge G_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon,(0)}(t,x),

for
n\geq 0
, we let

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}_{\Theta}

\displaystyle+\nabla_{x}\cdot\left(\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}v\left(w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}+G^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right)\right)=0

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}_{\Theta}

\displaystyle+\left[v\left(w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}+G^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right)\cdot\nabla_{x}\right]\left(w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}+G^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right)=v\left(w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}+G^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right)\wedge B^{\varepsilon,(n)},

with
\rho^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}_{\Theta}(0)\equiv\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}(0)
and
w^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}_{\Theta}(0)\equiv w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}(0)
, and where

\displaystyle G^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}

\displaystyle=G_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}+G_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}+G^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}_{\text{mean}}

with the terms in the decomposition given by

\displaystyle\widehat{G_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}}(t,k)

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{\varepsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}}\sin\left(\frac{s-\tau}{\varepsilon}\right)\widehat{g^{\varepsilon,(n)}}(\tau,k)d\tau ds+\widehat{G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},0}}(t,k),

\displaystyle\widehat{G_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}}(t,k)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{\mathrm{i}k\wedge\widehat{h^{\varepsilon,(n)}}(\tau,k)}{\varepsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}\sin\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}(s-\tau)}{\varepsilon}\right)d\tau ds+\widehat{G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},0}}(t,k),

and

\displaystyle G_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\sin\left(\frac{s-\tau}{\varepsilon}\right)q^{\varepsilon,(n)}(\tau)d\tau ds+G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},0}(t),

while

\displaystyle g^{\varepsilon,(n)}(t,x):=\partial_{x_{j}}\partial_{x_{i}}\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon,(n)}(t,x)v\left(\xi^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}\right)_{i}v\left(\xi^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}\right)_{j}d\mu(\Theta)

\displaystyle\qquad-\varepsilon^{2}\nabla_{x}\cdot\big(E^{\varepsilon,(n)}(t,x)\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon,(n)}(t,x)\big)-\nabla_{x}\cdot\left(j^{\varepsilon,(n)}(t,x)\wedge B^{\varepsilon,(n)}(t,x)\right)-\nabla_{x}\cdot R^{\varepsilon,(n)}(t,x),

\displaystyle h^{\varepsilon,(n)}(t,x):=\nabla_{x}\wedge\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}v\left(\xi^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}\right)_{i}v\left(\xi^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}\right)d\xi\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\varepsilon^{2}\nabla_{x}\wedge\big(E^{\varepsilon,(n)}(t,x)\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon,(n)}(t,x)\big)-\nabla_{x}\wedge(j^{\varepsilon,(n)}(t,x)\wedge B^{\varepsilon,(n)}(t,x))-\nabla_{x}\wedge R^{\varepsilon,(n)}(t,x),

and

\displaystyle q^{\varepsilon,(n)}(t)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon,(n)}(t,x)\nabla_{x}\cdot\big(\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon,(n)}(t,x)\big)dx

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}j^{\varepsilon,(n)}(t,x)\wedge B^{\varepsilon,(n)}(t,x)dx+\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\widehat{R^{\varepsilon,(n)}}(t,0),

and where
R^{\varepsilon,(n)}(t,x)
is defined as in (
3.15
), while

\displaystyle j^{\varepsilon,(n)}(t,x)=\int_{M}v\left(\xi^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}(t,x)\right)\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}(t,x)d\mu(\Theta),

and

\displaystyle E^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}

\displaystyle=E^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}_{\text{irr}}+E^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}_{\text{sol}}+E^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}_{\text{mean}}=\partial_{t}G_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}+\partial_{t}G_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}+\partial_{t}G_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)},

\displaystyle B^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}

\displaystyle=B^{\varepsilon}(0)-\nabla_{x}\wedge G_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}.

3.3.1
Estimates on the iterations

Lemma 3.1
.

There exist constants
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
independent of
\varepsilon
and
\Theta
such that, for
\eta
sufficiently small and independent of
\varepsilon
and
\Theta
, it holds that:

1.

For
n\geq 0

\displaystyle\max\left\{\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}},\left\lVert w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}},\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}},\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}},\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right\}\leq C_{1}.

(3.72)

2.

Moreover, for
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\max\left\{\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}-\rho^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}},\left\lVert w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}-w^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}},\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon,(n)}-G^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}},\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon,(n)}-\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}},\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon,(n)}-B^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right\}\leq\frac{C_{2}}{2^{n}}.

(3.73)

Proof of part (
1
) of Lemma
3.1
:
We prove the lemma using an inductive procedure, beginning with the case
n=0
.
By the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
, we know there exists a constant
C_{0}
independent of
\Theta
and
\varepsilon
such that
\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(0)\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq C_{0}
and
\left\lVert\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(0)\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq C_{0}
.
Since by definition
\rho^{\varepsilon,(0)}(t,x)\equiv\rho^{\varepsilon}(0,x)
, this immediately implies that
\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(0)\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq C_{0}
.

We now show how to obtain a bound on
\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon,(0)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}
, where we remind that

\displaystyle\widehat{G^{\varepsilon,(0)}}

\displaystyle(t,k)=\varepsilon\widehat{E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}}(0,k)\sin\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)+\varepsilon^{2}\widehat{\partial_{t}E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}}(0,k)\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}\widehat{E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}}(0,k)\sin\left(\frac{t\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{1+|k|^{2}}\widehat{\partial_{t}E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}}(0,k)\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{t\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon(2\pi)^{3}E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}(0)\sin\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)\mathbf{1}_{k=0}+\varepsilon^{2}(2\pi)^{3}\partial_{t}E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}(0)\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\mathbf{1}_{k=0}.

(3.74)

First, note that by recalling the expression of
\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}}(0)
in (
3.18
) and using the quasineutrality bound (
1.18
), we get

\displaystyle\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}(0)\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq\frac{\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon}(0)-1\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}}{\varepsilon}\leq C_{0}.

(3.75)

Next, by the expression of
\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}E_{\text{irr}}(0)
in (
3.18
), recalling that
j^{\varepsilon}(0)=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(0)v(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(0))\mu(d\Theta)
and by using inequality (
2.5
) for the relativistic velocity, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}(0)\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon}(0)\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq\sup_{\Theta}\left(\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(0)\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert v(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(0))\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right)\leq C_{0},

(3.76)

for some constant
C_{0}
independent of
\Theta
and
\varepsilon
.
For the solenoidal part, we use formula (
3.23
) to get

\displaystyle\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}(0)\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq\left\lVert\varepsilon\partial_{t}B^{\varepsilon}_{0}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}=\left\lVert\varepsilon\nabla_{x}\wedge E^{\varepsilon}_{0}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq C_{0},

(3.77)

for some constant
C_{0}
independent of
\Theta
and
\varepsilon
,
where we used that

\partial_{t}B^{\varepsilon}_{0}=-\nabla_{x}\wedge E^{\varepsilon}_{0}

and the initial assumption (
1.15
).
Moreover, again by formula (
3.23
), we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}}(0)\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq\left\lVert\nabla_{x}\wedge B^{\varepsilon}(0)\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\sup_{\Theta}\left(\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(0)\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert v(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(0))\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right)\leq C_{0},

(3.78)

where we used the initial assumption (
1.15
).
Finally, we study the spatial mean of
E^{\varepsilon}_{0}
. By the definition in (
3.28
) and the initial assumption (
1.15
), we get

\displaystyle|\varepsilon E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}(0)|\leq C_{0}.

(3.79)

Next, by the expression of
\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}
in (
3.28
), by using inequality (
2.5
) for the relativistic velocity, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}(0)\right|\leq\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon}(0)\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq\sup_{\Theta}\left(\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(0)\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert v(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(0))\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right)\leq C_{0}.

(3.80)

Therefore, by recalling (
3.74
) and collecting the estimates in (
3.75
), (
3.76
), (
3.77
), (
3.78
), (
3.79
) and (
3.80
) we get

\displaystyle\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon,(0)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}(0)\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}(0)\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}(0)\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}(0)\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\quad+C|\varepsilon E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}(0)|+C|\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}(0)|\leq C_{0},

for some constant
C_{0}
independent of
\Theta
and
\varepsilon
.
Similarly, since
E^{\varepsilon,(0)}=\partial_{t}G^{\varepsilon,(0)}
, we get that
\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon,(0)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}
is also uniformly bounded by a constant
C_{0}
independent of
\Theta
and
\varepsilon
.

Moreover, by the bound on
\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon,(0)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}
and since
w^{\varepsilon,(0)}_{\Theta}=\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(0)-G^{\varepsilon,(0)}
, we deduce that
\left\lVert w^{\varepsilon,(0)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}
is also bounded by
C_{0}
.

Finally, we deduce the same estimate on
\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon,(0)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}
by noticing that

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\wedge G_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon,(0)}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon\mathrm{i}k}{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}\wedge\widehat{E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}}(0,k)\sin\left(\frac{t\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\mathrm{i}k}{1+|k|^{2}}\wedge\widehat{\partial_{t}E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}}(0,k)\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{t\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right),

and since
\frac{\varepsilon\mathrm{i}k}{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}
and
\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\mathrm{i}k}{1+|k|^{2}}
are bounded in
k
, we can apply the same analysis as before.

Now, suppose for
n\geq 1
, the bounds (
3.72
) are true for a constant
C_{1}
defined by
C_{1}:=4C_{0}
. Then, we show by induction that the same bounds hold for
(n+1)
. Note that by the induction hypothesis at step
(n)
, the assumption in Lemma
2.1
is satisfied, and we can therefore use the a priori estimates from Section
3.1
and Section
3.2
.

By (
3.12
), we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}(0)\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+C\eta\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left(\left\lVert w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}+2CC_{1}^{2}\eta\leq C_{1},

where the last inequality follows by choosing
\eta
sufficiently small.
Similarly, by (
3.9
) and by taking
\eta
sufficiently small, we get

\displaystyle\left\lVert w^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}(0)\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+C\eta\left(\left\lVert w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right)\left(\left\lVert w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}+6CC_{1}^{2}\eta\leq C_{1}.

Next, recalling that
G^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}=G_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}+G_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}+G^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}_{\text{mean}}
and using the a priori estimates (
3.46
), (
3.63
) and (
3.70
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta\left[\sup_{\Theta}\left(\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}\right)+\ \left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon C\eta\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left(\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\sup_{\Theta}\left\lVert\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{3}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},0}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},0}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},0}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}}.

Hence, by the fact that
\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq CC_{1}^{2}
and by choosing
\eta
sufficiently small,

\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq C(C_{1}^{3}+C_{1}^{2}+C_{1}^{3})\eta+\varepsilon C(C_{1}^{4}+\varepsilon C_{1}^{4})\eta+3C_{0}\leq C_{1}.

Similarly, using (
3.47
), (
3.64
) and (
3.71
), we get

\displaystyle\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}}\leq C_{1}.

And finally, using (
3.65
), we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}.

This concludes the proof of the first part of the lemma, that is (
3.72
).

Proof of part (
2
) of Lemma
3.1
: For
n=1
, by choosing
C_{2}:=8C_{1}
and by triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\max

\displaystyle\left\{\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon,(1)}_{\Theta}-\rho^{\varepsilon,(0)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}},\left\lVert w^{\varepsilon,(1)}_{\Theta}-w^{\varepsilon,(0)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}},\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon,(1)}-G^{\varepsilon,(0)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}},\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon,(1)}-\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon,(0)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}},\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon,(1)}-B^{\varepsilon,(0)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right\}\leq 2C_{1}\leq\frac{C_{2}}{2}.

Therefore, we conclude that (
3.73
) is true for
n=1
. Now, we assume that (
3.73
) is true for step
(n)
, and we show by induction that the same estimates hold for
(n+1)
.
For
n\geq 2
, the continuity equation for the difference

\left(\rho^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}_{\Theta}-\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right)
is given by

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(\rho^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}_{\Theta}-\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right)+\nabla_{x}\cdot\left(\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}v\left(w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}+G^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right)-\rho^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}v\left(w^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}+G^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}\right)\right)=0.

We estimate the norm of the difference by adding and subtracting the mixed term, that is

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(\rho^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}_{\Theta}-\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right)+\nabla_{x}\cdot\left(\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}v\left(w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}+G^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right)-\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}v\left(w^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}+G^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla_{x}\cdot\left(\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}v\left(w^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}+G^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}\right)-\rho^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}v\left(w^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}+G^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}\right)\right)=0.

By applying similar inequality than the a priori estimate (
3.12
) and by triangle inequality, we get

\displaystyle\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}_{\Theta}-\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert v(w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}+G^{\varepsilon,(n)})-v(w^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}+G^{\varepsilon,(n-1)})\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\eta\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}-\rho^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert v(w^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}+G^{\varepsilon,(n-1)})\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}.

Then, using the two relativistic inequalities (
2.5
), (
2.8
) and the induction hypothesis (
3.73
), we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}_{\Theta}-\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left(\left\lVert w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}-w^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon,(n)}-G^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\eta\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}-\rho^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left(\left\lVert w^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2CC_{1}\frac{C_{2}}{2^{n}}\eta+2CC_{1}\frac{C_{2}}{2^{n}}\eta\leq\frac{C_{2}}{2^{n+1}},

where we used
\eta
sufficiently small.

Next, we estimate the difference for
w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}
. The equation for the difference is given by

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(w^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}_{\Theta}-w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}\right)

\displaystyle+\left[v\left(w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}+G^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right)\cdot\nabla_{x}\right]\left(w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}+G^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right)

\displaystyle-\left[v\left(w^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}+G^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}\right)\cdot\nabla_{x}\right]\left(w^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}+G^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}\right)

\displaystyle=v\left(w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}+G^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right)\wedge B^{\varepsilon,(n)}-v\left(w^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}+G^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}\right)\wedge B^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}.

We proceed as we did for
\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}
: we add and subtract the mixed terms in the second term of the r.h.s and also in the l.h.s, then we use the a priori estimate (
3.9
) and the triangle inequality. Hence, we deduce

\displaystyle\left\lVert w^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}_{\Theta}-w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{2}}{2^{n+1}}.

Finally, we estimate the difference for
G^{\varepsilon}
. First, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}-G^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert G_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}-G_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert G_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}-G_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert G_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}-G_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}}.

We have to estimate the norms on the r.h.s, so we start with the irrational term. By definition, the difference is given by

\displaystyle\widehat{G_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}}(t,k)-\widehat{G_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon,(n)}}(t,k)=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{-\mathrm{i}k}{\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}}\left(\widehat{g^{\varepsilon,(n)}}(\tau,k)-\widehat{g^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}}(\tau,k)\right)\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)d\tau.

We split this term as in (
3.30
) and using the same notation, we get

\displaystyle G_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}-G_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon,(n)}

\displaystyle=:I_{1}^{(n+1)}-I_{1}^{(n)}+I_{2}^{(n+1)}-I_{2}^{(n)}+I_{3}^{(n+1)}-I_{3}^{(n)}+I_{4}^{(n+1)}-I_{4}^{(n)}.

Next, we estimate the norm of each
\left(I_{j}^{(n+1)}-I_{j}^{(n)}\right)
. We use the same analysis as in Section
3.2
. We proceed as for
\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}
above: for
I_{1}
we add and subtract the mixed term and apply the a priori estimate (
3.34
). Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert I_{1}^{(n+1)}-I_{1}^{(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta\sup_{\Theta}\left(\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}-\rho^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\xi^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\eta\sup_{\Theta}\left(\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\xi^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}-\xi^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\xi^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}+\xi^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 4CC_{1}^{2}\frac{C_{2}}{2^{n}}\eta+8CC_{1}^{2}\frac{C_{2}}{2^{n}}\eta\leq\frac{C_{2}}{12\cdot 2^{n+1}},

(3.81)

where, for the penultimate inequality, we used that
\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}=w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+G^{\varepsilon}
, the induction hypothesis and we took
\eta
sufficiently small. Note that here, we explicitly required the constant to be
\frac{C_{2}}{12\cdot 2^{n+1}}
in order to be able to sum all the terms and finally get the right
\frac{C_{2}}{3\cdot 2^{n+1}}
for
\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}_{\text{irr}}-G^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\text{irr}}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}
.

Then, for
I_{2}
, we add and subtract the mixed term and we employ the a priori estimate (
3.38
). By the induction hypothesis and taking
\eta
sufficiently small, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert I_{2}^{(n+1)}-I_{2}^{(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\text{irr}}-\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\text{irr}}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\quad+C\eta\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\text{irr}}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon,(n)}-\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq 2CC_{1}\frac{C_{2}}{2^{n}}\eta\leq\frac{C_{2}}{12\cdot 2^{n+1}}.

(3.82)

For
I_{3}
, first note that by definition of
j^{\varepsilon}
we have
\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq C_{1}^{2}
and moreover by the relativistic inequality (
2.5
) and (
2.8
) we also get

\displaystyle\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon,(n)}-j^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert v(\xi^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta})-v(\xi^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta})\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert v(\xi^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta})\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}-\rho^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq CC_{1}\frac{C_{2}}{2^{n}}+CC_{1}\frac{C_{2}}{2^{n}}.

Therefore, by adding and subtracting the mixed term, using the a priori estimate (
3.41
), the induction hypothesis and taking
\eta
sufficiently small, we deduce

\displaystyle\left\lVert I_{3}^{(n+1)}-I_{3}^{(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon,(n)}-B^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+C\eta\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon,(n)}-j^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq CC_{1}^{2}\frac{C_{2}}{2^{n}}\eta+C\left(CC_{1}\frac{C_{2}}{2^{n}}+CC_{1}\frac{C_{2}}{2^{n}}\right)\eta\leq\frac{C_{2}}{12\cdot 2^{n+1}}.

(3.83)

Similarly, by using the a priori estimate (
3.45
) and recalling that
\lambda(\xi)=\nabla_{\xi}\left(v(\xi)-\xi\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert I_{4}^{(n+1)}-I_{4}^{(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta}-\rho^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left(\left\lVert E^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\lambda(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon,(n)})\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad+\left\lVert v(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon,(n)})\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\lambda(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon,(n)})\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\eta\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left\lVert\lambda(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon,(n)})\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\left\lVert E^{\varepsilon,(n)}-E^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}\right\rVert+\left\lVert v(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon,(n)})\wedge B^{\varepsilon,(n)}-v(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon,(n-1)})\wedge B^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\eta\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}_{\Theta}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\left(\left\lVert E^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}\right\rVert+\left\lVert v(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon,(n-1)})\wedge B^{\varepsilon,(n-1)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left\lVert\lambda(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon,(n)})-\lambda(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon,(n-1)})\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}.

By the relativistic inequalities (
2.5
), (
2.6
), (
2.8
), (
2.9
), the induction hypothesis, and by choosing
\eta
sufficiently small, we get

\displaystyle\left\lVert I_{4}^{(n+1)}-I_{4}^{(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{C_{2}}{2^{n}}(C_{1}+CC_{1}^{2})\varepsilon^{2}CC_{1}^{3}\eta+CC_{1}\varepsilon^{2}CC_{1}^{3}\left(\frac{C_{2}}{2^{n}}+CC_{1}\frac{C_{2}}{2^{n}}+C_{1}C\frac{C_{2}}{2^{n}}\right)\eta

\displaystyle\qquad+CC_{1}\left(C_{1}+CC_{1}^{2}\right)c\varepsilon^{2}\frac{C_{2}}{2^{n}}\eta\leq\frac{C_{2}}{12\cdot 2^{n+1}}.

(3.84)

Putting together (
3.81
), (
3.82
), (
3.83
), and (
3.84
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert G_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}-G_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}\leq\frac{C_{2}}{3\cdot 2^{n+1}}.

Similar estimates hold for
G_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}
and
G_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}
, thus

\displaystyle\left\lVert G^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}-G^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert G_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}-G_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert G_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}-G_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{\delta_{0}}+\left\lVert G_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}-G_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq 3\cdot\frac{C_{2}}{3\cdot 2^{n+1}}=\frac{C_{2}}{2^{n+1}}.

The same analysis applies to
\left(\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}-\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right)
and
\left(B^{\varepsilon,(n+1)}-B^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right)
and this concludes the proof of (
3.73
).

∎

3.4
Proof of Theorem
1.1

In this section, we conclude the proof of the local-in-time solutions to the Euler–Maxwell system (
1.12
) on a time interval
\eta>0
and independent of
\varepsilon
and with solutions uniformly bounded with respect to
\varepsilon
in the analytic norm.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.1
, we have showed that the sequences
\left(\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta},w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta},G^{\varepsilon,(n)},B^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right)
are bounded Cauchy sequences in
C\left([0,\eta];B_{\delta_{0}}\right)
for a given
\delta_{0}>1
. Therefore, there exist

\left(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta},w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta},G^{\varepsilon},B^{\varepsilon}\right)\in C\left([0,\eta];B_{\delta_{0}}\right)
such that

\displaystyle\left(\rho^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta},w^{\varepsilon,(n)}_{\Theta},G^{\varepsilon,(n)},B^{\varepsilon,(n)}\right)\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}\left(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta},w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta},G^{\varepsilon},B^{\varepsilon}\right).

By defining
\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}(t,x):=w_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}(t,x)+G^{\varepsilon}(t,x)
,
E^{\varepsilon}(t,x):=\partial_{t}G^{\varepsilon}
and letting
n
go to infinity in the definitions of sequences for our iterative scheme defined in Section
3.3
, we get that
\left(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta},\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta},E^{\varepsilon},B^{\varepsilon}\right)
are solutions to the Euler–Maxwell system (
1.12
) with initial data
(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(0),\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(0),E^{\varepsilon}(0),B^{\varepsilon}(0))
given by Theorem
1.1
.

∎

4
Derivation of the (e-MHD) system in the quasineutral limit

The goal of this section is to prove Theorem
1.2
. That is, we now want to consider the quasineutral limit as
\varepsilon
goes to zero and to derive the (e-MHD) system (
1.13
) starting from the Euler–Maxwell system (
1.12
).
We first introduce a suitable decomposition of the electric field that differs from the one that we already did
among spatial mean, irrotational and solenoidal parts.
The new splitting divides the electric field into two terms.
The first is oscillatory, of magnitude
\frac{1}{\varepsilon}
and leads to the momentum corrector, while the second is bounded.

To do this, given
\varphi,\psi\in C^{0}\left([0,T];H^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{x}^{d})\right)
for
d\in\mathbb{N}
,
s\geq 0
, and
t\in[0,T]
, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}_{1}\varphi(t,x)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2\pi\varepsilon}\int_{t}^{t+2\pi\varepsilon}\varphi(s,x)ds,

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}_{2}\psi(t,x)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Bigg(\Bigg\{\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{2\pi\varepsilon}\int_{t}^{t+\frac{2\pi\varepsilon}{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}}\widehat{\psi}(s,k)ds\Bigg\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\Bigg).

We hence introduce the following splitting for the electric field
E^{\varepsilon}
:

E^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=E^{\varepsilon}_{1}(t,x)+E^{\varepsilon}_{2}(t,x),\quad E^{\varepsilon}_{1}(t,x):=\left(Id-\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}\right)E^{\varepsilon}(t,x),\quad E^{\varepsilon}_{2}(t,x):=\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}(t,x),

(4.1)

where

\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}(t,x):=\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}_{1}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}}(t,x)+\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}_{2}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}}(t,x)+\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}_{1}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}(t).

To summarize, the electric field
E^{\varepsilon}
is decomposed into two terms,
E^{\varepsilon}_{1}
and
E^{\varepsilon}_{2}
. The latter is obtained by taking the time averages over the oscillation periods of the irrotational and solenoidal components of
E^{\varepsilon}
and of its spatial mean, while the former is its complementary part.

Unlike
\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}
,
which are the time averages of the electric field components with a
k
-independent oscillation frequency,
the solenoidal term
\mathcal{H}_{2}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}}
is treated differently. It is first averaged in Fourier space mode by mode and then inverted, since the dispersion relation of
E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}}
depends on the Fourier mode
k
.
Moreover notice that, since
E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}
is spatially homogeneous,
\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\varepsilon}E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}
depends only on time.

Proposition 4.1
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.2
, the following facts hold:

1.

There exists
C>0
such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|E^{\varepsilon}_{2}(t)\right\|_{H^{s-1}(\mathbb{T}_{x}^{3})}\leq C,\quad\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}_{1}(t)\right\|_{H^{s-1}(\mathbb{T}_{x}^{3})}\leq C.

2.

Defining the corrector

\displaystyle W^{\varepsilon}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{t}E^{\varepsilon}_{1}(s,x)ds=\int_{0}^{t}\left(\text{Id}-\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}\right)E^{\varepsilon}(s,x)ds,

(4.2)

there exists
C>0
such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|W^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{s-1}(\mathbb{T}_{x}^{3})}\leq C;\quad W^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup 0\,\text{weakly in }L^{2}_{t,x}.

Proof.

Part (
1
) of Proposition
4.1
: Let us first recall the formulas for
\widehat{E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}}
,
\widehat{E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}}
and
E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}
