given by (
3.16
), (
3.17
), (
3.21
), (
3.22
), and (
3.26
), (
3.27
)
that is

\displaystyle\widehat{E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}}(t,k)=-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{\varepsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}}\sin\left(\frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}\right)\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)ds+\widehat{E_{\text{irr},0}^{\varepsilon}}(t,k),

\displaystyle\widehat{E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}}(t,k)=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{\varepsilon|k|^{2}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}\sin\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}(t-s)}{\varepsilon}\right)\left(\mathrm{i}k\wedge\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)\right)ds+\widehat{E_{\text{sol},0}^{\varepsilon}}(t,k),

and

\displaystyle E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}(t)=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{t}\sin\left(\frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}\right)q^{\varepsilon}(s)ds+E_{\text{mean},0}^{\varepsilon}(t),

where

\widehat{E_{\text{irr},0}^{\varepsilon}}(t,k)=\widehat{E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}}(0,k)\cos\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)+\varepsilon\widehat{\partial_{t}E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}}(0,k)\sin\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right),

(4.3)

\widehat{E_{\text{sol},0}^{\varepsilon}}(t,k)=\widehat{E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}}(0,k)\cos\left(\frac{t\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)+\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}\widehat{\partial_{t}E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}}(0,k)\sin\left(\frac{t\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right),

(4.4)

E_{\text{mean},0}^{\varepsilon}(t)=E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}(0)\cos\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)+\varepsilon\partial_{t}E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}(0)\sin\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right),

(4.5)

and where
g^{\varepsilon},h^{\varepsilon}
and
q^{\varepsilon}
are defined in (
3.14
), (
3.20
) and (
3.25
).

We start studying
\left\lVert E_{2}^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\rVert_{H^{s-1}_{x}}
.
Since the time averages of the initial contributions in (
4.3
), (
4.4
) and (
4.5
) are vanishing, we get

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}}(t,k)=\widehat{\mathcal{I}_{\text{irr}}}(t,k)+\widehat{\mathcal{I}_{\text{sol}}}(t,k)+\mathcal{I}_{\text{mean}}(t)\mathbf{1}_{k=0},

where

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{I}_{\text{irr}}}(t,k)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{2\pi\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{t}^{t+2\pi\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{|k|^{2}}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\sin\left(\frac{s-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)d\sigma ds,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{I}_{\text{sol}}}(t,k)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2\pi\varepsilon^{2}|k|^{2}}\int_{t}^{t+\frac{2\pi\varepsilon}{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}}\int_{0}^{s}\left(\mathrm{i}k\wedge\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\right)\sin\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}(s-\sigma)}{\varepsilon}\right)d\sigma ds,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\text{mean}}(t)

\displaystyle:=\frac{(2\pi)^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{t}^{t+2\pi\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{s}q^{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\sin\left(\frac{s-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)d\sigma ds.

For the first integral, since

\int_{t}^{t+2\pi\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{|k|^{2}}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\sin\left(\frac{s-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)d\sigma ds=\int_{t}^{t+2\pi\varepsilon}\int_{t}^{s}\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{|k|^{2}}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\sin\left(\frac{s-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)d\sigma ds,

we get,
inverting the order of the integration,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{I}_{\text{irr}}}(t,k)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{t}^{t+2\pi\varepsilon}\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{|k|^{2}}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\int_{\sigma}^{t+2\pi\varepsilon}\sin\left(\frac{s-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)dsd\sigma

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi\varepsilon}\int_{t}^{t+2\pi\varepsilon}\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{|k|^{2}}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\left[1-\cos\left(\frac{t-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)\right]d\sigma.

(4.6)

Similarly, for the second integral, we get

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{I}_{\text{sol}}}(t,k)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi\varepsilon^{2}|k|^{2}}\int_{t}^{t+\frac{2\pi\varepsilon}{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}}\mathrm{i}k\wedge\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\int_{\sigma}^{t+\frac{2\pi\varepsilon}{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}}\sin\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}(s-\sigma)}{\varepsilon}\right)dsd\sigma

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi\varepsilon|k|^{2}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}\int_{t}^{t+\frac{2\pi\varepsilon}{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}}\mathrm{i}k\wedge\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\left[1-\cos\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}(t-\sigma)}{\varepsilon}\right)\right]d\sigma,

(4.7)

while concerning
\mathcal{I}_{\text{mean}}
, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\text{mean}}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{(2\pi)^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{t}^{t+2\pi\varepsilon}q^{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\int_{\sigma}^{t+2\pi\varepsilon}\sin\left(\frac{s-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)dsd\sigma=\frac{(2\pi)^{2}}{\varepsilon}\int_{t}^{t+2\pi\varepsilon}q^{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\left[1-\cos\left(\frac{t-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)\right]d\sigma.

(4.8)

By (
4.6
), (
4.7
) and (
4.8
), we arrive at

\left|\widehat{\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}}(t,k)\right|\leq\frac{C}{\pi\varepsilon}\int_{t}^{t+2\pi\varepsilon}\left[\frac{|\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)|}{|k|}+\mathbf{1}_{k=0}|q^{\varepsilon}(\sigma)|\right]d\sigma+\frac{1}{\pi\varepsilon|k|\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}\int_{t}^{t+\frac{2\pi\varepsilon}{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}}|\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)|d\sigma.

Recalling the assumption in (
1.16
), along with the definitions of
g^{\varepsilon}
,
h^{\varepsilon}
and
q^{\varepsilon}
in (
3.14
), (
3.20
) and (
3.25
), and using the algebra property of Sobolev spaces for
s-2>\frac{3}{2}
, we deduce that
g^{\varepsilon},h^{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
and
q^{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}_{t}
. This implies that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|E^{\varepsilon}_{2}(t)\right\|_{H^{s-1}(\mathbb{T}_{x}^{3})}\leq C.

The estimate on
\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{1}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}
follows from the identity
E^{\varepsilon}_{1}=E^{\varepsilon}-E^{\varepsilon}_{2}
and the assumption that
\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{x}}
is bounded by (
1.16
).

Part (
2
) of Proposition
4.1
: We start by writing explicitly
W^{\varepsilon}
:

\displaystyle\widehat{W^{\varepsilon}}(t,k)=\mathcal{J}_{0}(t,k)+\mathcal{J}_{1}(t,k)+\mathcal{J}_{2}(t,k)+\mathcal{J}_{3}(t)\mathbf{1}_{k=0}-\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)ds,

(4.9)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{0}(t,k)

\displaystyle:=\mathbf{1}_{k=0}(2\pi)^{3}\left[\varepsilon E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}(0)\sin\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)-\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}(0)\cos\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon\widehat{E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}}(0,k)\sin\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)-\varepsilon^{2}\widehat{\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}}}(0,k)\cos\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}\widehat{E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}}(0,k)\sin\left(\frac{t\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{1+|k|^{2}}\widehat{\partial_{t}E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}}(0,k)\cos\left(\frac{t\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{1}(t,k)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{\varepsilon|k|^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\sin\left(\frac{s-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)d\sigma ds,

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{2}(t,k)

\displaystyle:=\ \frac{1}{\varepsilon|k|^{2}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\left(\mathrm{i}k\wedge\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\right)\sin\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}(s-\sigma)}{\varepsilon}\right)d\sigma ds,

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{3}(t)

\displaystyle:=\frac{(2\pi)^{3}}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}q^{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\sin\left(\frac{s-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)d\sigma ds.

By the assumption (
1.16
) applied on the initial data, the six terms in
\mathcal{J}_{0}
are bounded in
L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}
: Indeed, by the expressions (
3.18
), (
3.23
) and (
3.28
) and reasoning as in (
3.75
), (
3.76
), (
3.77
), (
3.78
), (
3.79
) and (
3.80
), we get

\Big|\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}(0)\Big|\leq\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{s}_{x}},\quad\Big|\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}(0)\Big|\leq\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{s}_{x}},

\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{s-1}_{x}}\leq\varepsilon^{-1}\left\lVert\rho^{\varepsilon}(0)-1\right\rVert_{H^{s-2}_{x}}\leq\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{s-1}_{x}},\quad\left\lVert\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{s-1}_{x}}\leq\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{s-1}_{x}},

and

\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{s-1}_{x}}\leq\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\Big|\varepsilon E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}(0)\Big|,

\left\lVert\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{s-1}_{x}}\leq\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{s}_{x}}+\left\lVert j^{\varepsilon}(0)\right\rVert_{H^{s-1}_{x}}.

Moreover, all the terms in
\mathcal{J}_{0}
converge weakly to
0
in
L^{2}_{t,x}
.

We now study
\{\mathcal{J}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{3}
: By inverting the order of integration, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{1}(t,k)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{\varepsilon|k|^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\int_{\sigma}^{t}\sin\left(\frac{s-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)dsd\sigma

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{|k|^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\left[1-\cos\left(\frac{t-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)\right]d\sigma,

which is bounded in
L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}
, since
g^{\varepsilon}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
. Similarly

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{2}(t,k)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon|k|^{2}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{i}k\wedge\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\int_{\sigma}^{t}\sin\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}(s-\sigma)}{\varepsilon}\right)dsd\sigma

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1+|k|^{2})|k|^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{i}k\wedge\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\left[1-\cos\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}(t-\sigma)}{\varepsilon}\right)\right]d\sigma,

is bounded in
L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}
, since
h^{\varepsilon}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
.
Finally

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{3}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{(2\pi)^{3}}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{t}q^{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\int_{\sigma}^{t}\sin\left(\frac{s-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)dsd\sigma=(2\pi)^{3}\int_{0}^{t}q^{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\left[1-\cos\left(\frac{t-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)\right]d\sigma,

is also bounded uniformly in time, since
q^{\varepsilon}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}_{t}
.
By definition, we have

\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{H^{\varepsilon}}{E^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)ds=\int_{0}^{t}E^{\varepsilon}_{2}(s,k)ds,

and being
E_{2}^{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}
by part (
1
) of this proposition, it follows that
\int_{0}^{t}E_{2}^{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}
.
By (
4.9
), this proves there exists a constant
C>0
such that
\left\lVert W^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\leq C
.

We now prove that
W^{\varepsilon}(t,x)
weakly converge to
0
in
L^{2}_{t,x}
. We already observed that the initial contributions in
\mathcal{J}_{0}
weakly converge to
0
. Moreover, notice that

\mathcal{J}_{1}(t,k)=-\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{|k|^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)d\sigma+\mathcal{O}_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t,k),\quad\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(t,k):=\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{|k|^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\cos\left(\frac{t-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)d\sigma,

(4.10)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{2}(t,k)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1+|k|^{2})|k|^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{i}k\wedge\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)d\sigma+\mathcal{O}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(t,k),

(4.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(t,k)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{(1+|k|^{2})|k|^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\mathrm{i}k\wedge\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\right)\cos\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}(t-\sigma)}{\varepsilon}\right)d\sigma,

while

\mathcal{J}_{3}(t)=(2\pi)^{3}\int_{0}^{t}q^{\varepsilon}(\sigma)d\sigma+\mathcal{O}_{3}^{\varepsilon}(t),\quad\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}_{3}(t):=-(2\pi)^{3}\int_{0}^{t}q^{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\cos\left(\frac{t-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)d\sigma.

(4.12)

We now compute
\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)ds
integrating in time
(
4.6
), (
4.7
) and (
4.8
). Therefore, we get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)ds=-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{2\pi\varepsilon}\int_{s}^{s+2\pi\varepsilon}\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{|k|^{2}}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\left[1-\cos\left(\frac{s-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)\right]d\sigma ds

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\pi\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}|k|^{2}\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{s+\frac{2\pi\varepsilon}{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}}\mathrm{i}k\wedge\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\left[1-\cos\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}(s-\sigma)}{\varepsilon}\right)\right]d\sigma ds

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbf{1}_{k=0}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{(2\pi)^{2}}{\varepsilon}\int_{s}^{s+2\pi\varepsilon}q^{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\left[1-\cos\left(\frac{s-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)\right]d\sigma ds

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{|k|^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)ds+\frac{1}{(1+|k|^{2})|k|^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{i}k\wedge\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)ds+\mathbf{1}_{k=0}(2\pi)^{3}\int_{0}^{t}q^{\varepsilon}(s)ds+\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}(t,k),

(4.13)

where

\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}:=\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}_{1}+\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}_{2}+\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}_{3},

(4.14)

with

\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}_{1}:=\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{|k|^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)ds-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{2\pi\varepsilon}\int_{s}^{s+2\pi\varepsilon}\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{|k|^{2}}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\left[1-\cos\left(\frac{s-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)\right]d\sigma ds,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}^{\varepsilon}:

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{(1+|k|^{2})|k|^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{i}k\wedge\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{s+\frac{2\pi\varepsilon}{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}}\frac{\mathrm{i}k\wedge\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)}{2\pi\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}|k|^{2}\varepsilon}\left[1-\cos\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}(s-\sigma)}{\varepsilon}\right)\right]d\sigma ds,

and

\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}_{3}:=\mathbf{1}_{k=0}\left[-(2\pi)^{3}\int_{0}^{t}q^{\varepsilon}(s)ds+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{(2\pi)^{2}}{\varepsilon}\int_{s}^{s+2\pi\varepsilon}q^{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\left(1-\cos\left(\frac{s-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)d\sigma ds\right].

By summing (
4.10
), (
4.11
) and (
4.12
) and taking the difference with (
4.13
), we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{1}(t,k)+\mathcal{J}_{2}(t,k)+\mathcal{J}_{3}(t)\mathbf{1}_{k=0}-\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{H^{\varepsilon}}E^{\varepsilon}(s,k)ds=\mathcal{O}_{1}^{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{O}_{2}^{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{O}_{3}^{\varepsilon}-\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}

(4.15)

and, since they are oscillatory integrals,
\mathcal{O}_{1}^{\varepsilon}
,
\mathcal{O}_{2}^{\varepsilon}
and
\mathcal{O}_{3}^{\varepsilon}
weakly converge to
0
in
L^{2}_{t,x}
. Concerning
\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}
, we actually have strong convergence, indeed
\|\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\leq C\varepsilon
.
To see this, note that we can switch the order of integration in the second terms of
\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}_{1}
,
\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}_{2}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}_{3}
using that

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{s+2\pi\varepsilon}d\sigma ds=\int_{0}^{2\pi\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\sigma}dsd\sigma+\int_{2\pi\varepsilon}^{t}\int_{\sigma-2\pi\varepsilon}^{\sigma}dsd\sigma+\int_{t}^{t+2\pi\varepsilon}\int_{\sigma-2\pi\varepsilon}^{t}dsd\sigma.

Therefore, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{|k|^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)ds-\int_{0}^{2\pi\varepsilon}\frac{1}{2\pi\varepsilon}\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{|k|^{2}}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\int_{0}^{\sigma}\left[1-\cos\left(\frac{s-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)\right]dsd\sigma

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{2\pi\varepsilon}^{t}\frac{1}{2\pi\varepsilon}\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{|k|^{2}}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\int_{\sigma-2\pi\varepsilon}^{\sigma}\left[1-\cos\left(\frac{s-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)\right]dsd\sigma

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{t}^{t+2\pi\varepsilon}\frac{1}{2\pi\varepsilon}\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{|k|^{2}}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\int_{\sigma-2\pi\varepsilon}^{t}\left[1-\cos\left(\frac{s-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)\right]dsd\sigma.

By computing the three integrals in
ds
, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{|k|^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)ds-\int_{0}^{2\pi\varepsilon}\frac{1}{2\pi\varepsilon}\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{|k|^{2}}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\left(\sigma+\varepsilon\sin\left(\frac{-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)d\sigma-\int_{2\pi\varepsilon}^{t}\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{|k|^{2}}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)d\sigma

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{t}^{t+2\pi\varepsilon}\frac{1}{2\pi\varepsilon}\frac{\mathrm{i}k}{|k|^{2}}\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\left(t-\sigma+2\pi\varepsilon-\varepsilon\sin\left(\frac{t-\sigma}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)d\sigma.

Hence, we can bound
\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}_{1}
as follow,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}_{1}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|k|}\int_{0}^{2\pi\varepsilon}\left\lvert\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)\right\rvert ds+\frac{1}{|k|}\int_{0}^{2\pi\varepsilon}\frac{1}{2\pi\varepsilon}\left\lvert\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\right\rvert\left(2\pi\varepsilon+\varepsilon\right)d\sigma

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{|k|}\int_{t}^{t+2\pi\varepsilon}\frac{1}{2\pi\varepsilon}\left\lvert\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(\sigma,k)\right\rvert\left(2\pi\varepsilon+\varepsilon\right)d\sigma.

A similar estimate holds for both
\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}_{2}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}_{3}
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{|k|}\int_{0}^{2\pi\varepsilon}|\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)|ds+C\frac{1}{|k|}\int_{t}^{t+2\pi\varepsilon}|\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)|ds

\displaystyle\quad+C\frac{1}{(1+|k|^{2})|k|}\int_{0}^{\frac{2\pi\varepsilon}{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}}|\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)|ds+C\frac{1}{(1+|k|^{2})|k|}\int_{t}^{t+\frac{2\pi\varepsilon}{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}}|\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)|ds

(4.16)

\displaystyle\quad+C\mathbf{1}_{k=0}\left[\int_{0}^{2\pi\varepsilon}|q^{\varepsilon}(s)|ds+\int_{t}^{t+2\pi\varepsilon}|q^{\varepsilon}(s)|ds\right].

Since
q^{\varepsilon}
is uniformly bounded in time and
g^{\varepsilon}
and
h^{\varepsilon}
are bounded in
L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
, this proves that
\|\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\leq C\varepsilon
and concludes the proof.
∎

4.1
The limit (e-MHD) system

We now rigorously derive the limit (e-MHD) system (
1.13
).

Proposition 4.2
.

Given
0<\varepsilon<1
and
\Theta\in M
, let

w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x):=\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x)-W^{\varepsilon}(t,x),\quad b^{\varepsilon}(t,x):=B^{\varepsilon}(t,x)+\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon}(t,x)

(4.17)

where
W^{\varepsilon}
is the corrector defined in (
4.2
).
There exist a subsequence in
\varepsilon
and two vector fields
E,B:[0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
where
B
is solenoidal such that
w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta},\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}
(for every
\Theta\in M
) and
b^{\varepsilon}
converge in
C^{0}\left([0,T];H^{s^{\prime}-2}_{x}\right)
with
s^{\prime}<s
respectively to
w_{\Theta},\rho_{\Theta}
and
B
with

\partial_{t}w_{\Theta}+(w_{\Theta}\cdot\nabla_{x})w_{\Theta}=E+w_{\Theta}\wedge B,\quad\partial_{t}\rho_{\Theta}+\nabla_{x}\cdot\left(\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\right)=0;

\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}(t,x)d\mu(\Theta)=1,\quad\nabla_{x}\wedge E(t,x)=-\partial_{t}B(t,x),\quad\nabla_{x}\wedge B(t,x)=\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}(t,x)w_{\Theta}(t,x)\mu(d\Theta).

Proof.

Limit of the hydrodynamic quantities
w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta},\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}
: We begin by taking the limit of
w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}
.

By assumption (
1.16
), we have
\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{x}
, and by part (
2
) of Proposition
4.1
, we have
W^{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}
. Consequently, we obtain
w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}
. Moreover, since
\partial_{t}W^{\varepsilon}=E^{\varepsilon}_{1}
, the function
w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}
satisfies the equation

\partial_{t}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+(v(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+W^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{x})(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+W^{\varepsilon})=E^{\varepsilon}_{2}+v(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+W^{\varepsilon})\wedge B^{\varepsilon}.

By adding and subtracting the non relativistic velocity and rearranging terms, we rewrite this equation as follows

\partial_{t}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\cdot\nabla_{x})w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}=\left[E^{\varepsilon}_{2}-(W^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x})W^{\varepsilon}-W^{\varepsilon}\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon})\right]+w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\wedge b^{\varepsilon}+\bar{\mathcal{R}}^{\varepsilon}_{1}+\bar{\mathcal{R}}^{\varepsilon}_{2},

(4.18)

where
b^{\varepsilon}
is defined in (
4.17
), and the remainder terms are given by

\bar{\mathcal{R}}^{\varepsilon}_{1}:=-(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\cdot\nabla_{x})W^{\varepsilon}-(W^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x})w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+W^{\varepsilon}\wedge b^{\varepsilon}-w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon}),

\bar{\mathcal{R}}^{\varepsilon}_{2}:=\left[(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+W^{\varepsilon})-v(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+W^{\varepsilon})\right]\cdot\nabla_{x}(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+W^{\varepsilon})+\left[v(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+W^{\varepsilon})-(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+W^{\varepsilon})\right]\wedge B^{\varepsilon}.

We now study the terms on the r.h.s. in (
4.18
).
Since the term

E_{2}^{\varepsilon}-(W^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x})W^{\varepsilon}-W^{\varepsilon}\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon})

is bounded in
L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
, there exists a subsequence
\varepsilon^{\prime}
and a vector field
E\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
such that

E_{2}^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}-(W^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\cdot\nabla_{x})W^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}-W^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon^{\prime}})\rightharpoonup E

(4.19)

in the sense of distributions.

For the term
w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\wedge b^{\varepsilon}
, we recall that
w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}
, and from the definition (
4.17
), we deduce that
b^{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
because
B^{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{x}
by assumption (
1.16
) and
W^{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}
by part (
2
) of Proposition
4.1
.

Additionally, we compute

\partial_{t}b^{\varepsilon}=\partial_{t}B^{\varepsilon}+\nabla_{x}\wedge\partial_{t}W^{\varepsilon}=-\nabla_{x}\wedge E^{\varepsilon}+\nabla_{x}\wedge E^{\varepsilon}_{1}=-\nabla_{x}\wedge E^{\varepsilon}_{2}.

Since
E^{\varepsilon}_{2}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}
by part (
1
) of Proposition
4.1
, we conclude that
\partial_{t}b^{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
. Thus, there exists a subsequence
\varepsilon^{\prime}
and a solenoidal field
B\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
such that

b^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\to B\quad\text{in}\quad C^{0}([0,T];H^{s^{\prime}-2}_{x})\quad\text{for}\quad s^{\prime}<s.

Now, we analyze the remainder terms
\bar{\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{\varepsilon}
and
\bar{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{\varepsilon}
.
By Proposition
4.1
, since
W^{\varepsilon}
and
\partial_{x_{i}}W^{\varepsilon}
converge weakly to zero in
L^{2}_{t,x}
and
w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}
and
b^{\varepsilon}
are bounded in
L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
, it follows that
\bar{\mathcal{R}}^{\varepsilon}_{1}
converges weakly to zero in
L^{2}_{t,x}
,
since

(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\cdot\nabla_{x})W^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup 0,\quad(W^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x})w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\rightharpoonup 0,\quad W^{\varepsilon}\wedge b^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup 0,\quad\text{and}\quad w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup 0.

For the term
\bar{\mathcal{R}}^{\varepsilon}_{2}
, using the algebra property for Sobolev norm, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{\mathcal{R}}^{\varepsilon}_{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+W^{\varepsilon})-v(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+W^{\varepsilon})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}}\left\lVert w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+W^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\lVert v(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+W^{\varepsilon})-(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+W^{\varepsilon})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}}\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\left\lVert w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+W^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert^{3}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}}\left(\left\lVert w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+W^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\left\lVert B^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}}\right),

where in the last inequality we used Lemma
2.3
to treat the difference between the relativistic and non relativistic velocity.
Finally, since
w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}
and
W^{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}
and
B^{\varepsilon}\in L_{t}^{\infty}H_{x}^{s-2}
, we deduce that
\left\|\bar{\mathcal{R}}^{\varepsilon}_{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}}
goes to zero as
\varepsilon
goes to zero.

From (
4.18
), since all terms on the right-hand side are bounded in
L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
, it follows that
\partial_{t}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
. Thus, there exists a subsequence
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
dependent on
\Theta
such that

w^{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}_{\Theta}\to w_{\Theta}\quad\text{strongly in}\quad C^{0}([0,T];H^{s^{\prime}-2}_{x})\quad\text{for}\quad s^{\prime}<s.

We now consider the initial datum
w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(0)
. Since
W^{\varepsilon}(0,x)
, defined by (
4.9
), satisfies

\widehat{W^{\varepsilon}}(0,k)=-\varepsilon^{2}\widehat{\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}}}(0,k)-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{1+|k|^{2}}\widehat{\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}}}(0,k)-\mathbf{1}_{k=0}(2\pi)^{3}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}(0),

it follows that
W^{\varepsilon}(0)
is bounded in
H_{x}^{s-1}
. By the Kondrachov embedding theorem, there exists a subsequence
\varepsilon^{\prime}
such that
W^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}(0)
converges strongly in
H^{s^{\prime}-1}_{x}
for any
s^{\prime}<s
.

By assumption (
1.16
), we conclude that there exists
w_{\Theta}(0)\in H^{s^{\prime}-1}_{x}
such that

w^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}_{\Theta}(0)=\xi^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}_{\Theta}(0)-W^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}(0)\to w_{\Theta}(0),\quad\text{in}\quad H^{s^{\prime}-1}_{x}.

Moreover, the limit
w_{\Theta}(0)
does not depend on the subsequence
\varepsilon^{\prime}
since, in Theorem
1.2
, we assumed that the entire sequences
\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(0)
,
j^{\varepsilon}(0)
, and
B^{\varepsilon}(0)
weakly converge in
L^{2}_{x}
.

Taking the weak
L^{2}_{t,x}
limit of (
4.18
), we obtain

\partial_{t}w_{\Theta}+w_{\Theta}\cdot\nabla_{x}w_{\Theta}=E+w_{\Theta}\wedge B.

Since the solution of this equation is unique for fixed
E,B\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
, it follows that the limit solution
w_{\Theta}
is independent of the chosen subsequence. Thus, we can apply the same argument for all
\Theta\in M
.

For the continuity equation, by adding and subtracting
\nabla_{x}\cdot(\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon})
, we get

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}+\nabla_{x}\cdot(\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon})+\nabla_{x}\cdot\left(\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\left(v(\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon})-\xi_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\right)\right)=0.

We treat the third term as a remainder of order
\varepsilon^{2}
, using Lemma
2.3
for the relativistic velocity in Sobolev space.
Therefore, the derivation of the continuity equation in the limit follows similarly to the case of
w_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}
. As a result, we obtain that there exists
\rho_{\Theta}\in C([0,T];H^{s^{\prime}-2}_{x})
for
s^{\prime}<s
such that

\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}\to\rho_{\Theta}\quad\text{in}\quad C^{0}([0,T];H^{s^{\prime}-2}_{x}),

where
\rho_{\Theta}
satisfies the continuity equation

\partial_{t}\rho_{\Theta}+\nabla_{x}\cdot(\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta})=0.

Limit of the Maxwell system
:
By assumption (
1.16
), we have
\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}
. Recalling Gauss’s law,

\varepsilon^{2}\nabla_{x}\cdot E^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x)\mu(d\Theta)-1,

we deduce that
\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)-1
converges to zero in
L^{2}_{t,x}
. Since
\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}
strongly converges to
\rho_{\Theta}
in
L^{2}_{t,x}
, we obtain the neutrality condition

\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}(t,x)\mu(d\Theta)=1.

Next, we consider the Maxwell–Faraday equation. Rewriting it, we obtain

B^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=B^{\varepsilon}(0,x)-\int_{0}^{t}\nabla_{x}\wedge E^{\varepsilon}(s,x)ds=B^{\varepsilon}(0,x)-\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon}(t,x)-\int_{0}^{t}\nabla_{x}\wedge E^{\varepsilon}_{2}(s,x)ds.

Recalling that
b^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=B^{\varepsilon}(t,x)+\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon}(t,x)
, it follows that

b^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=B^{\varepsilon}(0,x)-\int_{0}^{t}\nabla_{x}\wedge E^{\varepsilon}_{2}(s,x)ds.

By assumption,
B^{\varepsilon}(0)
is bounded in
H^{s}_{x}
and converges weakly. Consequently, it converges in
H^{s^{\prime}}_{x}
to a limit
B(0)
for any
s^{\prime}<s
.
For the time integral of
\nabla_{x}\wedge E_{2}^{\varepsilon}
, we can rewrite it as

\int_{0}^{t}\nabla_{x}\wedge E^{\varepsilon}_{2}(s)ds=\int_{0}^{t}\nabla_{x}\wedge\left[E^{\varepsilon}_{2}(s)-(W^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x})W^{\varepsilon}(s)-W^{\varepsilon}\wedge\left(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon}(s)\right)\right]ds,

using the identity

\nabla_{x}\wedge((W^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x})W^{\varepsilon})+\nabla_{x}\wedge\left(W^{\varepsilon}\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon})\right)=0.

This follows from the vector calculus identity

(W^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x})W^{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{2}|W^{\varepsilon}|^{2}+(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon})\wedge W^{\varepsilon}.

Recalling (
4.19
), we pass to the limit in
L^{2}_{t,x}
and obtain

B(t,x)=B_{0}(x)-\int_{0}^{t}\nabla_{x}\wedge E(s,x)ds.

Finally, we analyze the Maxwell–Ampère law:

\nabla_{x}\wedge B^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}(t,x)+\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x)v(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x))\mu(d\Theta).

By adding and subtracting the
\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x)\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\wedge B^{\varepsilon}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}(t,x)+\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x)\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x)\mu(d\Theta)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x)\left(v(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x))-\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x)\right)\mu(d\Theta).

The last term can be treated as a remainder of order
\varepsilon^{2}
using Lemma
2.3
on the relativistic velocity for Sobolev spaces. Then, using the relation
b^{\varepsilon}=B^{\varepsilon}+\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon}
, we obtain

\nabla_{x}\wedge b^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x)w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x)\mu(d\Theta)+\nabla_{x}\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon})+\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,x)W^{\varepsilon}(t,x)+\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}.

Therefore, taking the
L^{2}_{t,x}
limit on both sides and using the fact that
W^{\varepsilon},\partial^{2}_{x_{i}}W^{\varepsilon}
, and
\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}
weakly converge to zero in
L^{2}_{t,x}
, we obtain

\nabla_{x}\wedge B(t,x)=\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}(t,x)w_{\Theta}(t,x)\mu(d\Theta).

∎

4.2
Correctors in the limit

In this section, we prove that as
\varepsilon
goes to zero, the corrector introduced in (
4.2
) has a limit that is the sum of six terms, which arise from the expressions for the spatial average, irrotational and solenoidal parts in which the electric field is decomposed. As will be clear in the proof, a similar limit decomposition also holds for
\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}
and
B^{\varepsilon}
. Indeed, as shown in Proposition
4.2
, the magnetic field
B^{\varepsilon}
also converges modulo the corrector
\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon}
, from which we obtain the limiting expression for the corrector of the magnetic field.

Given
\phi,\psi\in L^{\infty}\left([0,T];L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}_{x})\right)
for
d\in\mathbb{N}
, we define for
t\in[0,T]
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}\phi}(t,k)

\displaystyle:=\exp\left(\mp\mathrm{i}\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)\widehat{\phi}(t,k),

(4.20)

\displaystyle\widehat{T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\pm}\psi}(t,k)

\displaystyle:=\exp\left(\mp\mathrm{i}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)\widehat{\psi}(t,k).

We start by stating the following lemma, the proof of which can be found in
[
44
, Lemma 3.3.3]
.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
j\in\{1,2\}
, then the following results hold:

1.

T^{\varepsilon}_{j,+}

and
T^{\varepsilon}_{j,-}
are adjoints, they are isometries on
L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}
for any
s\geq 0
and

T_{j,-}^{\varepsilon}T_{j,+}^{\varepsilon}=T_{j,+}^{\varepsilon}T_{j,-}^{\varepsilon}=\text{Id}
;

2.

If
\phi^{\varepsilon}\to\phi
strongly in
L^{2}_{t,x}
, then
T_{j,+}^{\varepsilon}\phi^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup 0
weakly in
L^{2}_{t,x}
;

3.

If
\phi
and
\psi
are in
L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{x}
with
s>d/2
, then for
i,j\in\{1,2\}

T_{i,+}^{\varepsilon}\left(\phi T_{j,+}^{\varepsilon}\psi\right)\rightharpoonup 0
weakly in
L^{2}_{t,x}
.

We can now prove the main result of this section.

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
E^{\varepsilon}_{1}
and
W^{\varepsilon}
be defined as in (
4.1
) and (
4.2
) and
B
the limit magnetic field introduced in Proposition
4.2
.

Given
s^{\prime}<s
with
s>\frac{3}{2}+2
, there exist two spatially independent functions
d_{0,+},d_{0,-}\in C^{0}_{t}
, two irrotational components
d_{1,+},d_{1,-}\in C^{0}_{t}H^{s^{\prime}-1}_{x}
and two solenoidal components
d_{2,+},d_{2,-}\in C^{0}_{t}H^{s^{\prime}-1}_{x}
such that:

1.

\left\lVert\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}_{1}-T_{1,-}^{\varepsilon}d_{0,+}-T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}d_{0,-}-T_{1,-}^{\varepsilon}d_{1,+}-T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}d_{1,-}-T_{2,-}^{\varepsilon}d_{2,+}-T_{2,+}^{\varepsilon}d_{2,-}\right\rVert_{C^{0}_{t}H_{x}^{s^{\prime}-1}}\to 0;

2.

\left\lVert W^{\varepsilon}-T_{1,-}^{\varepsilon}\widetilde{d}_{0,+}-T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}\widetilde{d}_{0,-}-T_{1,-}^{\varepsilon}\widetilde{d}_{1,+}-T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}\widetilde{d}_{1,-}-T_{2,-}^{\varepsilon}\widetilde{d}_{2,+}-T_{2,+}^{\varepsilon}\widetilde{d}_{2,-}\right\rVert_{C^{0}_{t}H_{x}^{s^{\prime}-1}}\to 0
;

3.

\left\lVert\left(B^{\varepsilon}+T_{2,-}^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\wedge\widetilde{d}_{2,+}+T_{2,+}^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\wedge\widetilde{d}_{2,-}\right)-B\right\rVert_{C^{0}_{t}H_{x}^{s^{\prime}-2}}\to 0
;

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{d}_{0,\pm}(t)=\mp\mathrm{i}\,\,d_{0,\pm}(t),\qquad\widetilde{d}_{1,\pm}(t,x)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Big(\Big\{\mp\mathrm{i}\,\,\widehat{d_{1,\pm}}(t,k)\Big\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\Big)

(4.21)

\displaystyle\text{and}\quad\widetilde{d}_{2,\pm}(t,x)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Big(\Big\{\mp\mathrm{i}(1+|k|^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,\,\widehat{d_{2,\pm}}(t,k)\Big\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\Big).

Proof.

Part (
1
) of Proposition
4.4
. Given
\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}
we split it into
six
parts so that:

\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}=E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean,+}}+E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},-}+E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr,+}}+E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},-}+E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},+}+E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},-},

where, using Euler’s formula, we have

\displaystyle E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},\pm}(t)

\displaystyle:=\varepsilon\exp\left(\pm\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\right)\left[\frac{1}{2}E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}(0)\pm\frac{\varepsilon}{2\mathrm{i}}\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}(0)\pm\int_{0}^{t}\frac{q^{\varepsilon}(s)}{2\mathrm{i}\varepsilon}\exp\left(\mp\frac{\mathrm{i}s}{\varepsilon}\right)ds\right],

(4.22)

\displaystyle\widehat{E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},\pm}}(t,k)

\displaystyle:=\varepsilon\exp\left(\pm\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\right)\left[\frac{1}{2}\widehat{E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}}(0,k)\pm\frac{\varepsilon}{2\mathrm{i}}\widehat{\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}}}(0,k)\mp\int_{0}^{t}k\frac{\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)}{2|k|^{2}\varepsilon}\exp\left(\mp\frac{\mathrm{i}s}{\varepsilon}\right)ds\right],

(4.23)

and

\displaystyle\widehat{E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},\pm}}(t,k)

\displaystyle:=\varepsilon\exp\left(\pm\frac{\mathrm{i}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}t}{\varepsilon}\right)\Biggl[\frac{1}{2}\widehat{E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}}(0,k)\pm\frac{\varepsilon\widehat{\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}}}(0,k)}{2\mathrm{i}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}

(4.24)

\displaystyle\pm\int_{0}^{t}\frac{k\wedge\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)}{2|k|^{2}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}\varepsilon}\exp\left(\mp\frac{\mathrm{i}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}s}{\varepsilon}\right)ds\Biggr].

By part (
1
) of Lemma
4.3
, we have

\left\lVert T_{1,\pm}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},\pm}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}}=\left\lVert E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},\pm}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}}\leq C,\quad\left\lVert T_{1,\pm}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},\pm}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}=\left\lVert E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},\pm}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\leq C,

and

\left\lVert T_{2,\pm}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},\pm}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}=\left\lVert E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},\pm}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\leq C,

since
E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},\pm}
,
E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},\pm}
and
E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},\pm}
contain an extra factor of
\varepsilon
in the numerator, and thus the same estimates for
\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}_{1}
in Proposition
4.1
hold.

Moreover,

\partial_{t}T_{1,\pm}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},\pm}(t)=\pm\frac{q^{\varepsilon}(t)}{2\mathrm{i}}\exp\left(\mp\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\right),

\partial_{t}\hbox{\hskip 22.85942pt\hskip-22.85942pt\hbox{$T_{1,\pm}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},\pm}$}\hskip-22.85942pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.13344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 22.85942pt}(t,k)=\mp\frac{k\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(t,k)}{2|k|^{2}}\exp\left(\mp\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\right),

and

\partial_{t}\hbox{\hskip 23.31151pt\hskip-23.31151pt\hbox{$T_{2,\pm}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},\pm}$}\hskip-23.31151pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.13344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 23.31151pt}(t,k)=\pm\frac{k\wedge\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(t,k)}{2|k|^{2}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}\exp\left(\mp\frac{\mathrm{i}t\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\right).

By the already mentioned boundedness of
g^{\varepsilon}
,
h^{\varepsilon}
in
L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
and of
q^{\varepsilon}
in
L^{\infty}_{t}
, it follows that there exists
C>0
such that

\left\lVert\partial_{t}T_{1,\pm}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},\pm}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}}\leq C,\quad\left\lVert\partial_{t}T_{1,\pm}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},\pm}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s-1}}\leq C,\quad\left\lVert\partial_{t}T_{2,\pm}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},\pm}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s-1}}\leq C.

Hence, by compactness, there exist two spatially independent functions
d_{0,\pm}
, two irrotational components
d_{1,\pm}

and two solenoidal components
d_{2,\pm}
such that, up to a subsequence,

T_{1,\pm}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},\pm}\to d_{0,\pm}\quad\text{in}\quad C^{0}_{t},\quad\text{i.e.,}\quad E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},\pm}-T_{1,\mp}^{\varepsilon}d_{0,\pm}\to 0,

T_{1,\pm}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},\pm}\to d_{1,\pm}\quad\text{in}\quad C^{0}_{t}H^{s^{\prime}-1}_{x},\quad\text{i.e.,}\quad E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},\pm}-T_{1,\mp}^{\varepsilon}d_{1,\pm}\to 0,

and

T_{2,\pm}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},\pm}\to d_{2,\pm}\quad\text{in}\quad C^{0}_{t}H^{s^{\prime}-1}_{x},\quad\text{i.e.,}\quad E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},\pm}-T_{2,\mp}^{\varepsilon}d_{2,\pm}\to 0,

for
s^{\prime}<s
.
The statement follows from the decomposition
\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}_{1}(t,x)+\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}_{2}(t,x)
in (
4.1
) and the fact that
\left\lVert\varepsilon E_{2}^{\varepsilon}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\to 0

from part (
1
) of Proposition
4.1
.

Part (
2
) of Proposition
4.4
.
From the decomposition in (
4.9
) with (
4.15
), we recall that we can write

W^{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{J}_{0}^{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{O}_{1}^{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{O}_{2}^{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{O}_{3}^{\varepsilon}-\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon},

where
\mathcal{O}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\mathcal{O}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}_{3}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}
are defined in (
4.10
), (
4.11
), (
4.12
) and (
4.14
).
Using Euler’s formula again, we introduce the following splitting for
W^{\varepsilon}
:

W^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=W^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},+}(t)+W^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},-}(t)+W^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},+}(t,x)+W^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},-}(t,x)+W^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},+}(t,x)+W^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},-}(t,x)-\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}(t,x),

where

\displaystyle\widehat{W^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},\pm}}(t,k):=\varepsilon\exp\left(\pm\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\right)\left[\pm\frac{1}{2\mathrm{i}}\widehat{E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}}(0,k)-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\widehat{\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}}}(0,k)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{i}k\frac{\widehat{g^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)}{2|k|^{2}\varepsilon}\exp\left(\mp\frac{\mathrm{i}s}{\varepsilon}\right)ds\right],

(4.25)

\displaystyle\widehat{W^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},\pm}}(t,k)

\displaystyle:=\varepsilon\exp\left(\pm\frac{\mathrm{i}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}t}{\varepsilon}\right)\Biggl[\pm\frac{\widehat{E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}}(0,k)}{2\mathrm{i}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}-\frac{\varepsilon\widehat{\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}}}(0,k)}{2(1+|k|^{2})}

(4.26)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mathrm{i}k\wedge\widehat{h^{\varepsilon}}(s,k)}{2(1+|k|^{2})|k|^{2}\varepsilon}\exp\left(\mp\frac{\mathrm{i}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}s}{\varepsilon}\right)ds\Biggr],

and

\displaystyle W^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},\pm}(t):=\varepsilon\exp\left(\pm\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\right)\left[\pm\frac{1}{2\mathrm{i}}E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}(0)-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}(0)+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{q^{\varepsilon}(s)}{2\varepsilon}\exp\left(\mp\frac{\mathrm{i}s}{\varepsilon}\right)ds\right].

(4.27)

As for
E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},\pm}
,

E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},\pm}
and
E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},\pm}
, we deduce that
T_{1,\pm}^{\varepsilon}W^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},\pm}
,
\partial_{t}T_{1,\pm}^{\varepsilon}W^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},\pm}
and
T_{2,\pm}^{\varepsilon}W^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},\pm}
,
\partial_{t}T_{2,\pm}^{\varepsilon}W^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},\pm}
are bounded in
L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}
and
T_{1,\pm}^{\varepsilon}W^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},\pm}
,
\partial_{t}T_{1,\pm}^{\varepsilon}W^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},\pm}
are bounded in
L^{\infty}_{t}
. It follows there exist two spatially independent functions
\widetilde{d}_{0,\pm}
, two irrotational components
\widetilde{d}_{1,\pm}
and two solenoidal components
\widetilde{d}_{2,\pm}
such that

W^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},\pm}-T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\mp}\widetilde{d}_{0,\pm}\to 0\quad\text{in}\quad C^{0}_{t},

and, for
s^{\prime}<s
,

W^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},\pm}-T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\mp}\widetilde{d}_{1,\pm}\to 0\quad\text{in}\quad C^{0}_{t}H^{s^{\prime}-1}_{x},\quad

W^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},\pm}-T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\mp}\widetilde{d}_{2,\pm}\to 0\quad\text{in}\quad C^{0}_{t}H^{s^{\prime}-1}_{x}.

Since, by (
4.16
),
\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}
is converging to
0
strongly in
L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}
, the statement of part (
2
) of Proposition
4.1
follows.

Comparing expressions (
4.22
), (
4.23
), (
4.24
) and (
4.25
), (
4.26
), (
4.27
) we notice that

(\pm\mathrm{i})W^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},\pm}=E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean},\pm},\quad(\pm\mathrm{i})W^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},\pm}=E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr},\pm}\quad\text{and}\quad(\pm\mathrm{i})\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}W^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},\pm}=E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol},\pm}.

Hence we get the formula for
\widetilde{d}_{0,\pm}
,
\widetilde{d}_{1,\pm}
and
\widetilde{d}_{2,\pm}
given in (
4.21
).

Part (
3
) of Proposition
4.4
.
Recalling that
b^{\varepsilon}=B^{\varepsilon}+\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon}
, by the triangle inequality we have

\displaystyle\|B^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle+T_{2,-}^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\wedge\widetilde{d}_{2,+}+T_{2,+}^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\wedge\widetilde{d}_{2,-}-B\|_{C^{0}_{t}H_{x}^{s^{\prime}-2}}\leq\left\lVert b^{\varepsilon}-B\right\rVert_{C^{0}_{t}H_{x}^{s^{\prime}-2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\lVert\nabla_{x}\wedge\left(W^{\varepsilon}-T_{1,-}^{\varepsilon}\widetilde{d}_{0,+}-T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}\widetilde{d}_{0,-}-T_{1,-}^{\varepsilon}\widetilde{d}_{1,+}-T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}\widetilde{d}_{1,-}-T_{2,-}^{\varepsilon}\widetilde{d}_{2,+}-T_{2,+}^{\varepsilon}\widetilde{d}_{2,-}\right)\right\rVert_{C^{0}_{t}H_{x}^{s^{\prime}-2}},

(4.28)

where we used that

\nabla_{x}\wedge\widetilde{d}_{0,\pm}=\nabla_{x}\wedge\widetilde{d}_{1,\pm}=0
since
\widetilde{d}_{0,\pm}
are spatially independent and
\widetilde{d}_{1,\pm}
are irrotational.
Hence, since the first term on the r.h.s of (
4.28
) is going to zero by Proposition
4.2
and the second term is going to zero by part (
2
) of this proposition, we obtain the conclusion.
∎

4.3
Equation for the correctors

The goal here is to derive the equations satisfied by the correctors
d_{0,\pm}
,
d_{1,\pm}
and
d_{2,\pm}
. To find these equations, we take the weak limit in (
3.13
), (
3.19
) and (
3.24
). Specifically, for test functions
\phi_{1}\in C^{\infty}_{c}((0,T))
and
\phi_{2}\in C^{\infty}_{c}((0,T)\times\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x})
, we compute

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}\left((\varepsilon^{2}\partial^{2}_{tt}+\text{\rm Id})E_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}\right),\phi_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}q^{\varepsilon},\phi_{1}\rangle,

(4.29)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}\left((\varepsilon^{2}\partial^{2}_{tt}+\text{\rm Id})\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}\right),\phi_{2}\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}g^{\varepsilon},\phi_{2}\rangle,

(4.30)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\pm}\left(\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial^{2}_{tt}+(\text{\rm Id}-\Delta_{x})\right)\nabla_{x}\wedge E_{\text{sol}}^{\varepsilon}\right),\phi_{2}\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\pm}h^{\varepsilon},\phi_{2}\rangle,

(4.31)

where
g^{\varepsilon}
,
h^{\varepsilon}
and
q^{\varepsilon}
are defined in (
3.14
), (
3.20
) and (
3.25
).

The following proposition states the equations obtained by the limit correctors.

Proposition 4.5
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
1.2
, the equations satisfied by the correctors
d_{0,+}
,
d_{1,+}
and
d_{2,+}
are given by

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{i}\partial_{t}d_{0,+}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{\ell\in\boldsymbol{1}}\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,-\ell)\ell\cdot\widehat{d_{1,-}}(t,\ell)

(4.32)

\displaystyle+\mathrm{i}\Big[d_{0,+}(t)\wedge\Big(\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}B(0,x)dx\Big)+\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\widehat{d_{1,+}}(t,\ell)\wedge\widehat{B}(t,-\ell)\right)\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{\ell\in\boldsymbol{1}}\widehat{d_{1,-}}(t,-\ell)\wedge\left[\ell\wedge\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right],

and

\displaystyle-2k\cdot\partial_{t}\widehat{d_{1,+}}(t,k)=-2k_{i}k_{j}\int_{M}\hbox{\hskip 18.49165pt\hskip-18.49165pt\hbox{$\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}$}\hskip-18.49165pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.80013pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 18.49165pt}(t,k)\mu(d\Theta)(d_{0,+})_{j}(t)

(4.33)

\displaystyle+\frac{2\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}k_{i}k_{j}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\int_{M}(\widehat{\rho_{\Theta}{w_{\Theta}}})_{i}(t,k-\ell)\mu(d\Theta)\right)(\widehat{d_{1,+}})_{j}(t,\ell)

\displaystyle-2\delta_{k\in\boldsymbol{1}}k_{i}k_{j}(d_{0,-})_{i}(t)(\widehat{d_{2,+}})_{j}(t,k)\left(1+|k|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{3}}k_{i}k_{j}\sum_{\ell\in\boldsymbol{1}}(\widehat{d_{1,-}})_{i}(t,k-\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\widehat{d_{2,+}})_{j}(t,\ell)

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{3}}k_{i}k_{j}\sum_{\ell\in\Omega^{(1)}_{+,-}(k)}\left(1+|k-\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\widehat{d_{2,-}})_{i}(t,k-\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\widehat{d_{2,+}})_{j}(t,\ell)

\displaystyle+\frac{k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\cdot\sum_{\ell\in\boldsymbol{1}}\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)(k-\ell)\cdot\widehat{d_{1,-}}(t,k-\ell)

\displaystyle-k\cdot\left(d_{0,+}(t)\wedge\widehat{B}(t,k)\right)-\frac{k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\cdot\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\widehat{d}_{1,+}(t,\ell)\wedge\widehat{B}(t,k-\ell)

\displaystyle\-\displaystyle\delta_{k\in\boldsymbol{1}}k\cdot\left(d_{0,-}(t)\wedge\left[k\wedge\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,k)\left(1+|k|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\cdot\sum_{\ell\in\boldsymbol{1}}\widehat{d_{1,-}}(t,k-\ell)\wedge\left[\ell\wedge\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\cdot\sum_{\sigma\in\{\pm\}}\sum_{\ell\in\Omega^{(1)}_{\sigma,-\sigma}(k)}\left(1+|k-\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{d_{2,-\sigma}}(t,k-\ell)\wedge\left[\ell\wedge\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{d_{2,\sigma}}(t,\ell)\right],

and

\displaystyle-2

\displaystyle\left(1+|k|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}k\wedge\partial_{t}\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,k)=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\\
|\ell|=|k|\end{subarray}}k\wedge\left(k_{i}\int_{M}\hbox{\hskip 17.46065pt\hskip-17.46065pt\hbox{$\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}$}\hskip-17.46065pt\hskip-3.375pt\raisebox{9.84999pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-3.375pt\hskip 17.46065pt}(t,k-\ell)\mu(d\Theta)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{d}_{2,+}(t,\ell)\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\\
|\ell|=|k|\end{subarray}}k\wedge\left(k_{i}\int_{M}\hbox{\hskip 12.20981pt\hskip-12.20981pt\hbox{$\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}$}\hskip-12.20981pt\hskip-3.375pt\raisebox{6.97498pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-3.375pt\hskip 12.20981pt}(t,k-\ell)\mu(d\Theta)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\widehat{d}_{2,+})_{i}(t,\ell)\right)

\displaystyle+k\wedge\left(k_{i}((d_{0,+})_{i}(t)\widehat{d_{1,+}}(t,k)+d_{0,+}(t)(\widehat{d_{1,+}})_{i}(t,k))\right)\delta_{k\in\boldsymbol{1}}

\displaystyle+k\wedge\left(\frac{k_{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}(\widehat{d_{1,+}})_{i}(t,k-\ell)\widehat{d_{1,+}}(t,\ell)\right)\delta_{k\in\boldsymbol{1}}

\displaystyle-k\wedge\left(\frac{k_{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\sigma\in\{\pm\}}\sigma\sum_{\ell\in\Omega^{(2)}_{\sigma,-\sigma}(k)}\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}[(\widehat{d_{2,+}})_{i}(t,\ell)\widehat{d_{1,-\sigma}}(t,k-\ell)+(\widehat{d_{2,+}})(t,\ell)(\widehat{d_{1,-\sigma}})_{i}(t,k-\ell)]\right)

\displaystyle+k\wedge\left(d_{0,+}(t)k\cdot\widehat{d_{1,+}}(t,k)\right)\delta_{k\in\boldsymbol{1}}+k\wedge\left(\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\widehat{d_{1,+}}(t,k-\ell)\ell\cdot\widehat{d_{1,+}}(t,\ell)\right)\delta_{k\in\boldsymbol{1}}

\displaystyle+k\wedge\left(\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\sigma\in\{\pm\}}\sum_{\ell\in\Omega^{(2)}_{\sigma,-\sigma}(k)}\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)(k-\ell)\cdot\widehat{d_{1,-\sigma}}(t,k-\ell)\right)

\displaystyle-k\wedge\left(\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\\
|\ell|=|k|\end{subarray}}\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\left(1+\left\lvert\ell\right\rvert^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\wedge\widehat{B}(t,k-\ell)\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\\
|\ell|=|k|\end{subarray}}k\wedge\Biggl(\Bigl(\int_{M}\widehat{\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}}(t,k-\ell)d\mu(\Theta)\Bigr)\wedge\left[\ell\wedge\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right]\Biggr)

\displaystyle-k\wedge\left(\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\sigma\in\{\pm\}}\sigma\sum_{\ell\in\Omega^{(2)}_{\sigma,-\sigma}(k)}\widehat{d_{1,-\sigma}}(t,k-\ell)\wedge\left[\ell\wedge\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right]\right),

where
\boldsymbol{1}:=\{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}:|\ell|=\sqrt{3}\}
, and

\displaystyle\Omega^{(1)}_{\eta_{1},\eta_{2}}(k)

\displaystyle:=\{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\,:\,1+\eta_{1}\sqrt{1+|k-\ell|^{2}}+\eta_{2}\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}=0\},\quad\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\in\{\pm 1\},

(4.34)

\displaystyle\Omega^{(2)}_{\eta_{1},\eta_{2}}(k)

\displaystyle:=\{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\,:\,1+\eta_{1}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}+\eta_{2}\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}=0\},\quad\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\in\{\pm 1\}.

Similar equations hold for
d_{0,-}
,
d_{1,-}
and
d_{2,-}
.

Proof.

General idea of the proof.
To compute the weak limits of the integral quantities in (
4.30
) and (
4.31
), we use the Plancherel’s identity to express them in the Fourier variables.

It turns out that the quantities involved are oscillatory integrals with different phase functions due to the type of interaction of the electromagnetic waves.
The idea is to determine when the phase of the oscillatory integral is nonzero in order to identify which terms survive in the limit as
\varepsilon
goes to zero. Indeed, by the Riemann-Lebesgue lemma, when this phase is nonzero, the limit tends to zero.

When not specified, in this section the symbol
\underset{\varepsilon\to 0}{\lim}

refers to the weak limit.

Equation for the spatially homogeneous correctors
: We start studying the equation (
3.24
) for the spatially independent term
E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}
. Given
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}((0,T))
, we compute the l.h.s. of (
4.29
). By taking the adjoints of
T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}
and
\partial_{tt}^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{tt}^{2}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}

\displaystyle+E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}),\phi\rangle=\left\langle E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}},\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{tt}^{2}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}\phi+T_{1,-}^{\varepsilon}\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}\,,\,\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{tt}^{2}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}\phi+T_{1,-}^{\varepsilon}\phi\right\rangle=\left\langle\varepsilon T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}\,,\,\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Big(\varepsilon^{2}T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}\partial_{tt}^{2}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}\phi+\phi\Big)\right\rangle.

By part (
1
) of Proposition
4.4
, we have that
\varepsilon T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}\rightharpoonup d_{0,+}
in
L^{2}_{t}
, moreover

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon^{2}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\partial_{tt}^{2}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}\phi(t)+\phi(t)}{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\right)\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{tt}^{2}\left(\exp\left(\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\right)\phi(t)\right)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\phi(t)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\right)\left[-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\phi(t)+\frac{2\mathrm{i}}{\varepsilon}\partial_{t}\phi(t)+\partial_{tt}^{2}\phi(t)\right]\exp\left(\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\right)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\phi(t)=2\mathrm{i}\partial_{t}\phi(t)+\varepsilon\partial_{tt}^{2}\phi(t).

It follows that

\frac{\varepsilon^{2}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\partial_{tt}^{2}T_{1,-}^{\varepsilon}\phi+\phi}{\varepsilon}\to 2\mathrm{i}\partial_{t}\phi\quad\text{in}\quad L^{2}_{t}\quad\text{strongly}.

We conclude that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{tt}^{2}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}+E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}),\phi\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle d_{0,+},2\mathrm{i}\partial_{t}\phi\right\rangle=\left\langle 2\mathrm{i}\partial_{t}d_{0,+},\phi\right\rangle.

(4.35)

We now want to analyse the r.h.s. of (
4.29
).
Let us define

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}(t):=\fint_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}\varepsilon^{2}E^{\varepsilon}(t,x)\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}(t,x)dx,\quad\quad\mathcal{B}^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}(t):=\fint_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}j^{\varepsilon}(t,x)\wedge B^{\varepsilon}(t,x)dx,

(4.36)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}(t):=\fint_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}R^{\varepsilon}(t,x)dx,

where
\fint_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}=(2\pi)^{-3}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}
.
Therefore, we study

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}q^{\varepsilon},\phi\rangle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\langle T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}\left(\mathcal{E}_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}-\mathcal{B}_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}-\mathcal{R}_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}\right),\phi\rangle,

(4.37)

where
R^{\varepsilon}
is defined in (
3.15
).

Limit of the
\mathcal{E}_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}
term
: We start studying the first term in (
4.37
).
By part (
1
) of Proposition
4.4
, we have for
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}((0,T))

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\fint_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot(\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon})dx\right),\phi\right\rangle=

\displaystyle\qquad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\fint_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\sigma_{3}}({d_{0,-\sigma_{3}}})\nabla_{x}\cdot T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\sigma_{4}}({d_{1,-\sigma_{4}}})dx\right),\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma_{1}\in\{1,2\},\\
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\fint_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}T^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3}}({d_{\sigma_{1},-\sigma_{3}}})\nabla_{x}\cdot T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\sigma_{4}}({d_{1,-\sigma_{4}}})dx\right),\phi\right\rangle,

(4.38)

where we note that only
T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}({d_{1,\mp}})
appears with the divergence since
\nabla_{x}\cdot T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\pm}({d_{2,\mp}})=\nabla_{x}\cdot T^{\varepsilon}_{0,\pm}({d_{0,\mp}})=0
, as
d_{0,\mp}
are spatially independent and
d_{2,\mp}
are solenoidal.

The first term on the r.h.s. of (
4.38
) is vanishing by the divergence theorem, since
d_{0,\pm}
is spatially independent.
To compute the second term on the r.h.s. of (
4.38
), for
\sigma_{1}\in\{1,2\},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
and
\psi_{1}:=d_{\sigma_{1},-\sigma_{3}},\psi_{2}:=d_{1,-\sigma_{4}}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
, we look at which terms in

\mathcal{M}_{\sigma_{1},1,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t):=(2\pi)^{3}\mathcal{F}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3}}(\psi_{1})T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\sigma_{4}}(\nabla_{x}\cdot\psi_{2})\right)\right)(t,0)

give a non vanishing contribution as
\varepsilon
goes to zero.

If
\sigma_{1}=1
, using the formula for
T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}
given in (
4.20
), we have

\mathcal{M}_{1,1,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t)=\mathrm{i}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}[1+\sigma_{3}1+\sigma_{4}1]\right)\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,-\ell)\ell\cdot\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell),

with
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
. It follows that the phase of the oscillatory integrand
\mathcal{M}_{1,1,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}
is always non-zero
and therefore gives a vanishing contribution as
\varepsilon
goes to zero.

If
\sigma_{1}=2
we have

\mathcal{M}_{2,1,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t)=-\mathrm{i}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}[1+\sigma_{3}\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}+\sigma_{4}1]\right)\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,\ell)\ell\cdot\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,-\ell),

with
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
. We notice that the phase of the oscillatory integrand
\mathcal{M}_{2,1,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}
is non-zero, except when
\sigma_{3}=-,\sigma_{4}=+
so that the phase is zero for
|\ell|=\sqrt{3}
, i.e.,

\mathcal{M}_{2,1,-,+}(t)=-\mathrm{i}\sum_{\ell\in\boldsymbol{1}}\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,\ell)\ell\cdot\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,-\ell)+\text{oscillatory terms},

where
\boldsymbol{1}=\{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}:\ell_{i}\in\{\pm 1\},i\in\{1,2,3\}\}
.

Hence,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\fint_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}\left(E^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot(\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon})\right),\phi\right\rangle=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{\ell\in\boldsymbol{1}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,-\ell)\ell\cdot\widehat{d_{1,-}}(t,\ell)\phi(t)dt.

(4.39)

Limit of the
\mathcal{B}^{\varepsilon}_{\text{mean}}
term
: We now study the second term in (
4.37
), i.e., we consider

\mathcal{B}_{\text{mean}}^{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{B}_{\text{mean},1}^{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{B}_{\text{mean},2}^{\varepsilon},

where

\mathcal{B}_{\text{mean},1}^{\varepsilon}:=\fint_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge B^{\varepsilon}dx,\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{B}_{\text{mean},2}^{\varepsilon}:=\fint_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\left(v(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})-\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\right)\mu(d\Theta)\wedge B^{\varepsilon}dx.

The treatment of
\mathcal{B}_{\text{mean},2}^{\varepsilon}
is done using Lemma
2.3
on the difference between the relativistic and non relativistic velocity. Hence,
\mathcal{B}_{\text{mean},2}^{\varepsilon}
is a remainder term of order
\varepsilon^{2}
which is strongly converging to zero.
Therefore, we only focus on the
\mathcal{B}_{\text{mean},1}^{\varepsilon}
term. By recalling the two formulas in (
4.17
), we have

\mathcal{B}_{\text{mean},1}^{\varepsilon}=\fint_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+W^{\varepsilon})\mu(d\Theta)\wedge\left(b^{\varepsilon}(t,x)-\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon}\right)dx=:\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},1}+\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},2}+\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},3}+\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},4},

where

\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},1}:=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge b^{\varepsilon},\quad\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},2}:=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)W^{\varepsilon}\wedge b^{\varepsilon},

\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},3}:=-\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon}),\quad\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},4}:=-\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)W^{\varepsilon}\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon}).

For
\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},1}
, since
\fint\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge b^{\varepsilon}\to\fint\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge B
in
L^{2}
strongly by Proposition
4.2
, we obtain, using part (
2
) of Lemma
4.3
,

T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},1}=T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\fint_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge b^{\varepsilon}dx\right)\rightharpoonup 0\quad\text{in}\quad L^{2}_{t}.

Concerning
\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},2}
, we know
\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\to 1
and
b^{\varepsilon}\to B
in
L^{2}
strongly by Proposition
4.2
. Therefore, by adding and subtracting these two limits to
\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},2}
, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},2}=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\fint_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)W^{\varepsilon}\wedge b^{\varepsilon}dx\right)=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\fint_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}W^{\varepsilon}\wedge Bdx\right).

By part (
2
) of Proposition
4.4
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},2}=

\displaystyle\quad\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\fint_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}\left((T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{0,-})+(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{1,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}(\widetilde{d}_{2,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})\right)\wedge B\right).

However, by part (
3
) of Lemma
4.3
,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left[\fint_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{0,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{1,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}(\widetilde{d}_{2,-})\right)\wedge Bdx\right]=0.

Hence, we conclude that the following identity holds,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},2}=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left[\fint_{\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x}}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})\right)\wedge Bdx\right].

Taking
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}((0,T))
, we compute the weak limit of the first term in the last equation.
By using the expression for
T_{1,\pm}^{\varepsilon}
given by (
4.20
) and the one of
\widetilde{d}_{0,+}
given by (
4.21
), we obtain

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\fint T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})\wedge Bdx\right),\phi\right\rangle=-\mathrm{i}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}d_{0,+}(t)\wedge\Big(\fint B(t,x)dx\Big)\phi(t)dt,

(4.40)

which contributes to the limit equation.

Concerning the weak limit of the second term: By using the expression for
T_{1,\pm}^{\varepsilon}
given by (
4.20
) and the one of
\widetilde{d}_{1,+}
given by (
4.21
), we obtain

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\fint T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})\wedge Bdx\right),\phi\right\rangle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\widehat{d_{1,+}}(t,\ell)\wedge\widehat{B}(t,-\ell)\phi(t)dt,

(4.41)

which contributes to the limit equation. We now look at the weak limit of the third term, i.e.,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\fint T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})\wedge Bdx\right),\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\varepsilon}\left(-\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}+1\right)t\right)\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\wedge\widehat{B}(t,-\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\phi(t)dt.

Hence, the last integral is always of oscillatory type except when
\ell=0
, but in this case,
(\widehat{d_{2,+}})_{j}(t,0)=0
since it is solenoidal. It follows that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\fint T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})\wedge Bdx\right),\phi\right\rangle=0.

We conclude that the contributions for
\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},2}
are given by (
4.40
) and (
4.41
). Hence

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}

\displaystyle\left\langle T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},2},\phi\right\rangle=-\mathrm{i}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\left[d_{0,+}(t)\wedge\Big(\fint B(t,x)dx\Big)+\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\widehat{d_{1,+}}(t,\ell)\wedge\widehat{B}(t,-\ell)\right)\right]\phi(t)dt.

(4.42)

Next, we study
\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},3}
. Since
\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\to\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)
, by adding and subtracting the limit, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},3}=-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\fint\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon})dx\right).

Next, by part (
2
) of Proposition
4.4
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},3}=

\displaystyle-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\fint\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge\left(\nabla_{x}\wedge\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{0,-}+\widetilde{d}_{1,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+}+\widetilde{d}_{1,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}(\widetilde{d}_{2,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})\right)\right)dx\right).

Observe that
\nabla_{x}\wedge\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{0,-}+\widetilde{d}_{1,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+}+\widetilde{d}_{1,+})\right)=0
since
\widetilde{d}_{0,\pm}
are spatially independent and
\widetilde{d}_{1,\pm}
are irrotational. Moreover, by part (
3
) of Lemma
4.3
and by commuting the curl with
T_{2,+}^{\varepsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\fint\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}(\nabla_{x}\wedge\widetilde{d}_{2,-})\right)dx\right)=0.

Hence, the following identity holds:

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},3}=-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\fint\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\nabla_{x}\wedge\widetilde{d}_{2,+})dx\right).

Taking
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}((0,T))
and
\psi(t,x):=\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
, we compute the weak limit of the last equation. By the expression for
T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}
,
T_{2,-}^{\varepsilon}
and the one of
\widetilde{d}_{2,+}
given by (
4.21
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\fint\psi\wedge T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\nabla_{x}\wedge\widetilde{d}_{2,+})dx\right),\phi\right\rangle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\widehat{\psi}(t,-\ell)\wedge\left[\ell\wedge\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right]\exp\left(\mathrm{i}\frac{\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}t}{\varepsilon}\right)\phi(t)dt

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\exp\left(\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\varepsilon}\left(\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}-1\right)t\right)\widehat{\psi}(t,-\ell)\wedge\left[\ell\wedge\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right]\phi(t)dt.

Hence, the limit is vanishing except when
\ell=0
, which gives a zero contribution.

We finally study
\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},4}
. We know that
\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\to 1
in
L^{2}
strongly by Proposition
4.2
. Therefore, by adding and subtracting the limit, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},4}=-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\fint\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)W^{\varepsilon}\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon})dx\right)=-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\fint W^{\varepsilon}\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon})dx\right).

By part (
2
) of Proposition
4.4
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\fint W^{\varepsilon}\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon})dx\right),\phi\right\rangle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\sigma_{3}}(\widetilde{d}_{0,-\sigma_{3}})\wedge\left(\fint\nabla_{x}\wedge T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\sigma_{4}}(\widetilde{d}_{2,-\sigma_{4}})dx\right)\right),\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma_{1}\in\{1,2\},\\
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\fint T^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3}}(\widetilde{d}_{\sigma_{1},-\sigma_{3}})\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\sigma_{4}}(\widetilde{d}_{2,-\sigma_{4}}))dx\right),\phi\right\rangle,

(4.43)

where we note that only
T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\pm}({d_{2,\mp}})
appears with the curl operator since
\nabla_{x}\wedge T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}({d_{1,\mp}})=\nabla_{x}\wedge T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}({d_{0,\mp}})=0
, as
d_{0,\mp}
are spatially independent and
d_{1,\mp}
are irrotational.

The first term on the r.h.s. of (
4.43
) is vanishing by the Stokes’ theorem. Concerning the second term on the r.h.s of (
4.43
) we study, for
\sigma_{1}\in\{1,2\},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
and
\psi_{1}:=\widetilde{d}_{\sigma_{1},-\sigma_{3}},\psi_{2}:=\widetilde{d}_{2,-\sigma_{4}}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
, which terms in

\mathcal{\bar{B}}_{\sigma_{1},2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t):=(2\pi)^{3}\mathcal{F}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\fint T^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3}}(\psi_{1})\wedge T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\sigma_{4}}(\nabla_{x}\wedge\psi_{2})dx\right)\right)(t,0)

give a non vanishing contribution as
\varepsilon
goes to zero.

If
\sigma_{1}=1
, using the formula for
T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}
and
T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\pm}
given in (
4.20
), we have

\mathcal{\bar{B}}_{1,2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t)=\mathrm{i}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}[1+\sigma_{3}1+\sigma_{4}\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}]\right)\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,-\ell)\wedge[\ell\wedge\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell)],

with
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
. We notice that the phase of the oscillatory integrand
\mathcal{\bar{B}}_{1,2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}
is non-zero, except when
\sigma_{3}=+,\sigma_{4}=-
so that the phase is zero for
|\ell|=\sqrt{3}
, i.e.,

\mathcal{\bar{B}}_{1,2,+,-}(t)=\mathrm{i}\sum_{\ell\in\boldsymbol{1}}\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,-\ell)\wedge[\ell\wedge\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell)]+\text{oscillatory terms},

where
\boldsymbol{1}=\{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}:\ell_{i}\in\{\pm 1\},i\in\{1,2,3\}\}.

If
\sigma_{1}=2
we have

\mathcal{\bar{B}}_{2,2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t)=\mathrm{i}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\left[1+\sigma_{3}\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}+\sigma_{4}\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}\right]\right)\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,-\ell)\wedge[\ell\wedge\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell)],

with
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
. We notice that the phase of the oscillatory integrand
\mathcal{B}_{2,2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}
is always non-zero.

We conclude that

\displaystyle-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{A}_{\text{m},4},\phi\right\rangle=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\sum_{\ell\in\boldsymbol{1}}\widehat{d_{1,-}}(t,-\ell)\wedge\left[\ell\wedge\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right]\phi(t)dt.

(4.44)

The study of the fourth term in (
4.37
), which corresponds to the remainder, follows by using the Sobolev version of inequality (
2.6
) in Lemma
2.2
and is converging to zero.

Conclusion
: Finally, by collecting (
4.35
), (
4.39
), (
4.42
), and (
4.44
) we get the equation satisfied by the corrector
d_{0,+}
given by (
4.32
).

Equation for the irrotational correctors
: We now study the equation (
3.13
) for the irrotational part
E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}}
. Given
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}((0,T)\times\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x})
, we compute the l.h.s. of (
4.30
). By taking the adjoints of
T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}
,
\partial_{tt}^{2}
and the divergence operator, we have

\displaystyle\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{tt}^{2}\nabla_{x}\cdot E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}}

\displaystyle+\nabla_{x}\cdot E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}}),\phi\rangle=\left\langle\nabla_{x}\cdot E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}},\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{tt}^{2}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}\phi+T_{1,-}^{\varepsilon}\phi\right\rangle=-\left\langle E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}},\nabla_{x}\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{tt}^{2}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}\phi+T_{1,-}^{\varepsilon}\phi\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}},\nabla_{x}\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{tt}^{2}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}\phi+T_{1,-}^{\varepsilon}\phi\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\varepsilon T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}},\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\left(\varepsilon^{2}T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}\partial_{tt}^{2}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}\phi+\phi\right)\right\rangle,

where in the last equality we used that
\nabla_{x}
commute with
T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}
.

By part (
1
) of Proposition
4.4
, we have that
\varepsilon T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}}\rightharpoonup d_{1,+}
in
L^{2}_{t,x}
, moreover

\displaystyle\frac{\hbox{\hskip 31.87856pt\hskip-31.87856pt\hbox{$\varepsilon^{2}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\partial_{tt}^{2}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}\phi$}\hskip-31.87856pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.44016pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 31.87856pt}+\widehat{\phi}}{\varepsilon}(t,k)=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\right)\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{tt}^{2}\left(\exp\left(\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\right)\widehat{\phi}(t,k)\right)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\widehat{\phi}(t,k)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\right)\left[-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\widehat{\phi}(t,k)+\frac{2\mathrm{i}}{\varepsilon}\partial_{t}\widehat{\phi}(t,k)+\partial_{tt}^{2}\widehat{\phi}(t,k)\right]\exp\left(\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\right)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\widehat{\phi}(t,k)

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{i}\partial_{t}\widehat{\phi}(t,k)+\varepsilon\partial_{tt}^{2}\widehat{\phi}(t,k).

It follows that

\frac{\hbox{\hskip 31.87856pt\hskip-31.87856pt\hbox{$\varepsilon^{2}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\partial_{tt}^{2}T_{1,-}^{\varepsilon}\phi$}\hskip-31.87856pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.44016pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 31.87856pt}+\widehat{\phi}}{\varepsilon}\to 2\mathrm{i}\partial_{t}\widehat{\phi}\quad\text{in}\quad L^{2}_{t}H^{1}_{x}\quad\text{strongly}.

We conclude that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{tt}^{2}\nabla_{x}\cdot E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}}+\nabla_{x}\cdot E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{irr}}),\phi\rangle

\displaystyle=-\left\langle d_{1,+},\nabla_{x}\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\left\{2\mathrm{i}\partial_{t}\widehat{\phi}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\right)\right\rangle=\left\langle 2\mathrm{i}\partial_{t}\nabla_{x}\cdot(d_{1,+}),\phi\right\rangle.

(4.45)

We now want to analyse the r.h.s. of (
4.30
).
Let us define
