\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}(t,x):=\partial_{x_{i}}\left(\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}^{\varepsilon}v(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})_{i}v(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})\mu(d\Theta)\right),\quad\quad\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(t,x):=\varepsilon^{2}E^{\varepsilon}(t,x)\left(\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon}(t,x)\right),

(4.46)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\varepsilon}(t,x):=j^{\varepsilon}(t,x)\wedge B^{\varepsilon}(t,x).

Therefore, we study

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}g^{\varepsilon},\phi\rangle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\langle T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}\left(\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}(t,x)-\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(t,x)-\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathcal{B}^{\varepsilon}(t,x)-\nabla_{x}\cdot R^{\varepsilon}(t,x)\right),\phi\rangle,

(4.47)

where
R^{\varepsilon}
is defined in (
3.15
).

Limit of the
\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}
term
: We study the first term in (
4.47
), i.e., we consider

\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=\sum_{i,j=1}^{3}\partial_{x_{i}}\partial_{x_{j}}\mathcal{J}_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t,x)+\sum_{i,j=1}^{3}\partial_{x_{i}}\partial_{x_{j}}\mathcal{J}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(t,x),

where

\mathcal{J}_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t,x):=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x)(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x))_{i}(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x))_{j}\mu(d\Theta)

and

\mathcal{J}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(t,x):=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x)[v(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x))_{i}v(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x))_{j}-(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x))_{i}(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x))_{j}]\mu(d\Theta),

for
i,j\in\{1,2,3\}
. The treatment of
\mathcal{J}_{2}^{\varepsilon}
is done using Lemma
2.3
on the difference between the relativistic and non relativistic velocity. Therefore,
\mathcal{J}_{2}^{\varepsilon}
is a remainder term of order
\varepsilon^{2}
which is strongly converging to zero.

By the definition of
w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}=\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}-W^{\varepsilon}
and (
4.2
), we have

\mathcal{J}_{1}^{\varepsilon}=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}((w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})_{i}+W^{\varepsilon}_{i})((w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})_{j}+W^{\varepsilon}_{j})\mu(d\Theta)=:\mathcal{A}^{i,j}_{1}+\mathcal{A}^{i,j}_{2}+\mathcal{A}^{j,i}_{2}+\mathcal{A}^{i,j}_{3},

where

\mathcal{A}^{i,j}_{1}:=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})_{i}(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})_{j}\mu(d\Theta),\,\,\mathcal{A}_{2}^{i,j}:=W^{\varepsilon}_{j}\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})_{i}\mu(d\Theta),\,\,\mathcal{A}^{i,j}_{3}:=W^{\varepsilon}_{i}W^{\varepsilon}_{j}\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta).

For
\mathcal{A}^{i,j}_{1}
, since
\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})_{i}(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})_{j}\mu(d\Theta)\to\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}(w_{\Theta})_{j}\mu(d\Theta)
in
L^{2}
strongly by Proposition
4.2
, we obtain, using part (
2
) of Lemma
4.3
,

T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\mathcal{A}^{i,j}_{1}=T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})_{i}(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})_{j}\mu(d\Theta)\right)\rightharpoonup 0\quad\text{in}\quad L^{2}_{t,x}.

Concerning
\mathcal{A}^{i,j}_{2}
, since
\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})_{i}\mu(d\Theta)\to\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}\mu(d\Theta)
in
L^{2}
strongly by Proposition
4.2
, we get by adding and subtracting
\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}\mu(d\Theta)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\mathcal{A}^{i,j}_{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})_{i}-\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}\mu(d\Theta)\right)W^{\varepsilon}_{j}

\displaystyle\quad+\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}\mu(d\Theta)\right)W^{\varepsilon}_{j}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}\mu(d\Theta)\right)W^{\varepsilon}_{j}.

(4.48)

We now remind that, by part (
2
) Proposition
4.4
, we have

W^{\varepsilon}_{j}-T_{1,-}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})_{j}-T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{d}_{0,-})_{j}-T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})_{j}-T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{1,-})_{j}-T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})_{j}-T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}(\widetilde{d}_{2,-})_{j}\to 0\quad\text{in}\quad C^{0}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x},

where
\widetilde{d}_{0,\pm}
,
\widetilde{d}_{1,\pm}
and
\widetilde{d}_{2,\pm}
are defined in (
4.21
). Therefore, we can rewrite the last limit as

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\mathcal{A}^{i,j}_{2}=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}\mu(d\Theta)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left(T_{1,-}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})_{j}-T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{d}_{0,-})_{j}+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})_{j}+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{1,-})_{j}+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})_{j}+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}(\widetilde{d}_{2,-})_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}\mu(d\Theta)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left(W^{\varepsilon}_{j}-T_{1,-}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})_{j}-T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{d}_{0,-})_{j}-T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})_{j}-T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{1,-})_{j}-T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})_{j}-T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}(\widetilde{d}_{2,-})_{j}\right),

(4.49)

and the last term is going to zero thanks to part (
2
) of Proposition
4.4
.
Moreover, by part (
3
) of Lemma
4.3
, we notice that,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left[\left(\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}\mu(d\Theta)\right)\left(T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{d}_{0,-})_{j}+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{1,-})_{j}+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}(\widetilde{d}_{2,-})_{j}\right)\right]=0.

We conclude that the following identity holds:

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\mathcal{A}^{i,j}_{2}=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left[\left(\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}\mu(d\Theta)\right)\left(T_{1,-}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})_{j}+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})_{j}+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})_{j}\right)\right].

(4.50)

Taking
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}((0,T)\times\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x})
and defining
\psi(t,x):=\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}\mu(d\Theta)\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
, we compute the weak limit of the first term in (
4.50
). That is,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\psi T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})_{j}\right),\phi\right\rangle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle\psi(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})_{j}\,,\,\phi\right\rangle

(4.51)

where we used that
d_{0,+}
is spatially homogeneous and that
T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}=\text{Id}
. Therefore, this term contributes to the limit equation.
Then, we compute the weak limit of the second term in (
4.50
). By using Plancherel identity, the expression for
T_{1,\pm}^{\varepsilon}
given by (
4.20
) and the one of
\widetilde{d}_{1,+}
given by (
4.21
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\psi T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})_{j}\right),\phi\right\rangle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp{\left(\frac{-\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\right)}\hbox{\hskip 30.70236pt\hskip-30.70236pt\hbox{$\left(\psi T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})_{j}\right)$}\hskip-30.70236pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.80013pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 30.70236pt}(t,k)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt

\displaystyle\qquad=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp{\left(\frac{-\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\right)}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\widehat{\psi}(t,k-\ell)\exp{\left(\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\right)}\widehat{(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})_{j}}(t,\ell)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt

\displaystyle\qquad=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\sum_{k,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\widehat{\psi}(t,k-\ell)(\widehat{d_{1,+}})_{j}(t,\ell)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt.

(4.52)

Therefore, this term contributes to the limit equation.
We now look at the weak limit of the third term in (
4.50
), i.e.,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\psi T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})_{j}\right),\phi\right\rangle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\widehat{\psi}(t,k-\ell)\exp\left(\mathrm{i}\frac{\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}t}{\varepsilon}\right)(\widehat{d_{2,+}})_{j}(t,\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt

\displaystyle\quad=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\exp\left(\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\varepsilon}\left(\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}-1\right)t\right)\widehat{\psi}(t,k-\ell)(\widehat{d_{2,+}})_{j}(t,\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt.

Hence, the last integral is always of oscillatory type except when
\ell=0
, but in this case
(\widehat{d_{2,+}})_{j}(t,0)=0
since it is solenoidal. It follows that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\psi T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})_{j}\right),\phi\right\rangle=0.

We conclude that the contributions for
\mathcal{A}_{2}^{i,j}
are given by (
4.51
) and (
4.52
). Hence

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}

\displaystyle\left\langle T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x_{i}}\partial_{x_{j}}(\mathcal{A}_{2}^{i,j}+\mathcal{A}_{2}^{j,i}),\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}k_{i}k_{j}\left(\int_{M}\hbox{\hskip 18.49165pt\hskip-18.49165pt\hbox{$\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}$}\hskip-18.49165pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.80013pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 18.49165pt}(t,k)\mu(d\Theta)(d_{0,+})_{j}(t)\right)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{2\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{k,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}k_{i}k_{j}\left(\int_{M}\hbox{\hskip 18.49165pt\hskip-18.49165pt\hbox{$\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}$}\hskip-18.49165pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.80013pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 18.49165pt}(t,k-\ell)\mu(d\Theta)(\widehat{d}_{1,+})_{j}(t,\ell)\right)\bar{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt.

(4.53)

Finally, we study
\mathcal{A}^{i,j}_{3}
. We know that
\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\to 1
in
L^{2}
strongly by Proposition
4.2
. Therefore, by adding and subtracting the limit as in (
4.48
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\mathcal{A}^{i,j}_{3}=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(W^{\varepsilon}_{i}W^{\varepsilon}_{j}\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\right)=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(W^{\varepsilon}_{i}W^{\varepsilon}_{j}\right).

By part (
2
) of Proposition
4.4
and proceeding as in (
4.49
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(W^{\varepsilon}_{i}W^{\varepsilon}_{j}\right),\phi\right\rangle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\{0,1,2\},\\
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3}}(\widetilde{d}_{\sigma_{1},-\sigma_{3}})_{i}T^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{2},\sigma_{4}}(\widetilde{d}_{\sigma_{2},-\sigma_{4}})_{j}\right),\phi\right\rangle.

where we defined
T_{0,\pm}^{\varepsilon}=T_{1,\pm}^{\varepsilon}
for notational convenience.
We now study, for
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\{0,1,2\}
,
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
and
\psi_{1}:=(\widetilde{d}_{\sigma_{1},-\sigma_{3}})_{i},\psi_{2}:=(\widetilde{d}_{\sigma_{2},-\sigma_{4}})_{j}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
, which terms in

\mathcal{J}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k):=\mathcal{F}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3}}(\psi_{1})T^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{2},\sigma_{4}}(\psi_{2})\right)\right)(t,k)

give a non vanishing contribution as
\varepsilon
goes to zero.

If
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\{0,1\}
, using the formula for
T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}
given in (
4.20
), we have

\mathcal{J}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k)=\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}[1+\sigma_{3}1+\sigma_{4}1]\right)\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,k-\ell)\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell),

with
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
. It follows that, for
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\{0,1\}
, the phase of the oscillatory integrand
\mathcal{J}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}
is always non-zero and therefore gives a vanishing contribution as
\varepsilon
goes to zero.

If
\sigma_{1}=0
and
\sigma_{2}=2
, we get

\mathcal{J}_{0,2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}[1+\sigma_{3}1+\sigma_{4}\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}]\right)\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,k-\ell)\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell),

with
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
. We notice that the phase of the oscillatory integrand
\mathcal{J}_{0,2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}
is non-zero, except when
\sigma_{3}=+,\sigma_{4}=-
so that the phase is zero for
|\ell|=\sqrt{3}
, i.e.,

\mathcal{J}_{0,2,+,-}(t,k)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\boldsymbol{1}}\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,k-\ell)\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell)+\text{oscillatory terms}

where
\boldsymbol{1}=\{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}:\ell_{i}\in\{\pm 1\},i\in\{1,2,3\}\}
. The analogous conclusion holds for
\mathcal{J}_{2,0,-,+}

If
\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}=2
, we can assume w.l.o.g. that
\sigma_{1}=1,\sigma_{2}=2
and we get

\mathcal{J}_{1,2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}[1+\sigma_{3}1+\sigma_{4}\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}]\right)\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,k-\ell)\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell),

with
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
. We notice that the phase of the oscillatory integrand
\mathcal{J}_{1,2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}
is non-zero, except when
\sigma_{3}=+,\sigma_{4}=-
so that the phase is zero for
|\ell|=\sqrt{3}
, i.e.,

\mathcal{J}_{1,2,+,-}(t,k)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\boldsymbol{1}}\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,k-\ell)\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell)+\text{oscillatory terms}

where
\boldsymbol{1}=\{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}:\ell_{i}\in\{\pm 1\},i\in\{1,2,3\}\}
. The analogous conclusion holds for
\mathcal{J}_{2,1,-,+}
.

If
\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{2}=2
we have

\mathcal{J}_{2,2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}[1+\sigma_{3}\sqrt{1+|k-\ell|^{2}}+\sigma_{4}\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}]\right)\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,k-\ell)\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell),

with
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
. We notice that the phase of the oscillatory integrand
\mathcal{J}_{2,2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}
is non-zero, except when
\sigma_{3}\sigma_{4}=-
. Hence

\mathcal{J}_{2,2,\sigma_{3},-\sigma_{3}}(t,k)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\Omega^{(1)}_{\sigma_{3},-\sigma_{3}}(k)}\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,k-\ell)\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell)+\text{oscillatory terms},

where
\Omega^{(1)}_{\sigma_{3},-\sigma_{3}}(k)
is defined by (
4.34
).

Collecting the limit contributions given by
\mathcal{J}_{0,2,+,-},\mathcal{J}_{2,0,-,+},

\mathcal{J}_{1,2,+,-},\mathcal{J}_{2,1,-,+},\mathcal{J}_{2,2,+,-}
and
\mathcal{J}_{2,2,-,+}
we arrive at

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x_{i}}\partial_{x_{j}}\mathcal{A}_{3}^{i,j},\phi\right\rangle=-\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{k\in\boldsymbol{1}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}k_{i}k_{j}\left((d_{0,-})_{i}(t)(\widehat{d_{2,+}})_{j}(t,k)\left(1+|k|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\,\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}k_{i}k_{j}\left(\sum_{\ell\in\boldsymbol{1}}(\widehat{d_{1,-}})_{i}(t,k-\ell)(\widehat{d_{2,+}})_{j}(t,\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\,\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}k_{i}k_{j}\Bigg(\sum_{\ell\in\Omega^{(1)}_{+,-}(k)}(\widehat{d_{2,-}})_{i}(t,k-\ell)\left(1+|k-\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\widehat{d_{2,+}})_{j}(t,\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg)\,\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt.

(4.54)

Limit of the
\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}
term
: We now study the second term in (
4.47
).
By part (
1
) of Proposition
4.4
and proceeding as in (
4.49
), we have for
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}((0,T)\times\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x})

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot(\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon})\right),\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\sigma_{3}}({d_{0,-\sigma_{3}}})\nabla_{x}\cdot T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\sigma_{4}}({d_{1,-\sigma_{4}}})\right),\phi\right\rangle

(4.55)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma_{1}\in\{1,2\},\\
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3}}({d_{\sigma_{1},-\sigma_{3}}})\nabla_{x}\cdot T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\sigma_{4}}({d_{1,-\sigma_{4}}})\right),\phi\right\rangle,

where we note that only
T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}({d_{1,\mp}})
appears with the divergence since
\nabla_{x}\cdot T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}({d_{0,\mp}})=\nabla_{x}\cdot T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\pm}({d_{2,\mp}})=0
, as as
d_{0,\mp}
are spatially independent and
d_{2,\mp}
are solenoidal.

The first term on the r.h.s. of (
4.55
) is vanishing since the oscillatory phase is always non-zero.
To compute the second term on the r.h.s. of (
4.55
), for
\sigma_{1}\in\{1,2\},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
and
\psi_{1}:=d_{\sigma_{1},-\sigma_{3}},\psi_{2}:=d_{1,-\sigma_{4}}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
, we look at which terms in

\mathcal{E}_{\sigma_{1},1,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k):=\mathcal{F}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3}}(\psi_{1})T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\sigma_{4}}(\nabla_{x}\cdot\psi_{2})\right)\right)(t,k)

give a non vanishing contribution as
\varepsilon
goes to zero.

If
\sigma_{1}=1
, using the formula for
T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}
given in (
4.20
), we have

\mathcal{E}_{1,1,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k)=\mathrm{i}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}[1+\sigma_{3}1+\sigma_{4}1]\right)\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,k-\ell)\ell\cdot\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell),

with
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
. It follows that the phase of the oscillatory integrand
\mathcal{E}_{1,1,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}
is always non-zero
and therefore gives a vanishing contribution as
\varepsilon
goes to zero.

If
\sigma_{1}=2
we have

\mathcal{E}_{2,1,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k)=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}[1+\sigma_{3}\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}+\sigma_{4}1]\right)\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,\ell)(k-\ell)\cdot\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,k-\ell),

with
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
. We notice that the phase of the oscillatory integrand
\mathcal{E}_{2,1,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}
is non-zero, except when
\sigma_{3}=-,\sigma_{4}=+
so that the phase is zero for
|\ell|=\sqrt{3}
, i.e.,

\mathcal{E}_{2,1,-,+}(t,k)=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\boldsymbol{1}}\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,\ell)(k-\ell)\cdot\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,k-\ell)+\text{oscillatory terms},

where
\boldsymbol{1}=\{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}:\ell_{i}\in\{\pm 1\},i\in\{1,2,3\}\}
.

Hence,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}-\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\nabla_{x}\cdot\left(E^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot(\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon})\right),\phi\right\rangle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\boldsymbol{1}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}k\cdot\left(\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)(k-\ell)\cdot\widehat{d_{1,-}}(t,k-\ell)\right)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt.

(4.56)

Limit of the
\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathcal{B}^{\varepsilon}
term
: We now study the third term in (
4.47
), i.e., we consider

\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathcal{B}^{\varepsilon}=\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\varepsilon}+\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\varepsilon},

where

\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\varepsilon}:=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge B^{\varepsilon},\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\varepsilon}:=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\left(v(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})-\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\right)\mu(d\Theta)\wedge B^{\varepsilon}.

The treatment of
\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\varepsilon}
is done using Lemma
2.3
on the difference between the relativistic and non relativistic velocity. Therefore,
\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\varepsilon}
is a remainder term of order
\varepsilon^{2}
which is strongly converging to zero.
Therefore, we only focus on the
\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\varepsilon}
term. By recalling the two formulas in (
4.17
), we have

\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\varepsilon}=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+W^{\varepsilon})\mu(d\Theta)\wedge\left(b^{\varepsilon}(t,x)-\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon}\right)=:\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}+\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2}+\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{3}+\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{4},

where

\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}:=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge b^{\varepsilon},\quad\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2}:=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)W^{\varepsilon}\wedge b^{\varepsilon},

\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{3}:=-\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon}),\quad\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{4}:=-\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)W^{\varepsilon}\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon}).

For
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
, since
\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge b^{\varepsilon}\to\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge B
in
L^{2}
strongly by Proposition
4.2
, we obtain, using part (
2
) of Lemma
4.3
,

T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}=T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge b^{\varepsilon}\right)\rightharpoonup 0\quad\text{in}\quad L^{2}_{t,x}.

Concerning
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, we know
\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\to 1
and
b^{\varepsilon}\to B
in
L^{2}
strongly by Proposition
4.2
. Therefore, similarly to (
4.48
) by adding and subtracting these two limits to
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2}=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)W^{\varepsilon}\wedge b^{\varepsilon}\right)=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(W^{\varepsilon}\wedge B\right).

By part (
2
) of Proposition
4.4
and similarly as in (
4.49
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2}=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{0,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{1,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}(\widetilde{d}_{2,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})\right)\wedge B\right).

However, by part (
3
) of Lemma
4.3
,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left[\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{0,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{1,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}(\widetilde{d}_{2,-})\right)\wedge B\right]=0.

Hence, we conclude that the following identity holds,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2}=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left[\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})\right)\wedge B\right].

(4.57)

Taking
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}((0,T)\times\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x})
, we compute the weak limit of the first term in (
4.57
). By the expression for
T_{1,\pm}^{\varepsilon}
given by (
4.20
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})\wedge B\right),\phi\right\rangle=\left\langle\widetilde{d}_{0,+}\wedge B,\phi\right\rangle

(4.58)

where we used that
d_{0,+}
is spatially homogeneous. Then, we compute the weak limit of the second term in (
4.57
). By using again Plancherel identity, the expression for
T_{1,\pm}^{\varepsilon}
given by (
4.20
) and the one of
\widetilde{d}_{1,+}
given by (
4.21
), we obtain

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})\wedge B\right),\phi\right\rangle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\sum_{k,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\widehat{d_{1,+}}(t,\ell)\wedge\widehat{B}(t,k-\ell)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt,

(4.59)

which contributes to the limit equation. We now look at the weak limit of the second term, i.e.,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})\wedge B\right),\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\varepsilon}\left(-\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}+1\right)t\right)\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\wedge\widehat{B}(t,k-\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt.

Hence, the last integral is always of oscillatory type except when
\ell=0
, but in this case,
(\widehat{d_{2,+}})_{j}(t,0)=0
since it is solenoidal. It follows that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})\wedge B\right),\phi\right\rangle=0.

We conclude that the contributions for
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
are given by (
4.58
) and
(
4.59
). Hence

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}-\left\langle T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2},\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}k\cdot\left(d_{0,+}(t)\wedge\widehat{B}(t,k)\right)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{k,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}k\cdot\left(\widehat{d_{1,+}}(t,\ell)\wedge\widehat{B}(t,k-\ell)\right)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt.

(4.60)

Next, we study
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{3}
. Since
\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\to\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)
, by adding and subtracting the limit, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{3}=-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon})\right).

Next, by part (
2
) of Proposition
4.4
and similarly as in (
4.49
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{3}

\displaystyle=-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\wedge\left(\nabla_{x}\wedge\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{0,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{1,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}(\widetilde{d}_{2,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})\right)\right)\right).

Observe that
\nabla_{x}\wedge\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{0,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{1,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})\right)=0
since
\widetilde{d}_{0,\pm}
are spatially homogeneous
\widetilde{d}_{1,\pm}
are irrotational. Moreover, by by part (
3
) of Lemma
4.3
and by commuting the curl with
T_{2,+}^{\varepsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}(\nabla_{x}\wedge\widetilde{d}_{2,-})\right)\right)=0.

Hence, the following identity holds:

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{3}=-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\nabla_{x}\wedge\widetilde{d}_{2,+})\right).

Taking
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}((0,T)\times\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x})
and
\psi(t,x):=\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
, we compute the weak limit of the last equation. By using Plancherel identity, the expression for
T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}
,
T_{2,-}^{\varepsilon}
and the one of
\widetilde{d}_{2,+}
given by (
4.21
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\psi\wedge T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\nabla_{x}\wedge\widetilde{d}_{2,+})\right),\phi\right\rangle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\widehat{\psi}(t,k-\ell)\wedge\left[\ell\wedge\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right]\exp\left(\mathrm{i}\frac{\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}t}{\varepsilon}\right)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\exp\left(\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\varepsilon}\left(\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}-1\right)t\right)\widehat{\psi}(t,k-\ell)\wedge\left[\ell\wedge\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right]\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt.

Hence, the limit is vanishing except when
\ell=0
, which gives a zero contribution.

We finally study
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{4}
. We know that
\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\to 1
in
L^{2}
strongly by Proposition
4.2
. Therefore, by adding and subtracting the limit as in (
4.48
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{4}=-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)W^{\varepsilon}\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon})\right)=-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(W^{\varepsilon}\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon})\right).

By part (
2
) of Proposition
4.4
and proceeding as in (
4.49
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(W^{\varepsilon}\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon})\right),\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma_{1}\in\{0,1,2\},\\
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3}}(\widetilde{d}_{\sigma_{1},-\sigma_{3}})\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\sigma_{4}}(\widetilde{d}_{2,-\sigma_{4}}))\right),\phi\right\rangle,

(4.61)

where we introduced the notation
T^{\varepsilon}_{0,+}:=T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}
. We note that only
T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\pm}({d_{2,\mp}})
appears with the curl operator since
\nabla_{x}\wedge T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}({d_{0,\mp}})=\nabla_{x}\wedge T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}({d_{1,\mp}})=0
, as
d_{0,\mp}
are spatially homogeneous and
d_{1,\mp}
are irrotational.

We study for
\sigma_{1}\in\{0,1,2\},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
and
\psi_{1}:=\widetilde{d}_{\sigma_{1},-\sigma_{3}},\psi_{2}:=\widetilde{d}_{2,-\sigma_{4}}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
, which terms in

\mathcal{B}_{\sigma_{1},2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k):=\mathcal{F}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3}}(\psi_{1})\wedge T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\sigma_{4}}(\nabla_{x}\wedge\psi_{2})\right)\right)

give a non vanishing contribution as
\varepsilon
goes to zero.

If
\sigma_{1}\in\{0,1\}
, using the formula for
T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}
and
T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\pm}
given in (
4.20
), we have

\mathcal{B}_{\sigma_{1},2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k)=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}[1+\sigma_{3}1+\sigma_{4}\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}]\right)\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,k-\ell)\wedge[\ell\wedge\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell)],

with
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
. We notice that the phase of the oscillatory integrand
\mathcal{B}_{\sigma_{1},2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}
is non-zero, except when
\sigma_{3}=+,\sigma_{4}=-
so that the phase is zero for
|\ell|=\sqrt{3}
, i.e.,

\mathcal{B}_{\sigma_{1},2,+,-}(t,k)=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\boldsymbol{1}}\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,k-\ell)\wedge[\ell\wedge\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell)]+\text{oscillatory terms},

where
\boldsymbol{1}=\{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}:\ell_{i}\in\{\pm 1\},i\in\{1,2,3\}\}.

If
\sigma_{1}=2
we have

\mathcal{B}_{2,2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k)=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\left[1+\sigma_{3}\sqrt{1+|k-\ell|^{2}}+\sigma_{4}\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}\right]\right)\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,k-\ell)\wedge[\ell\wedge\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell)],

with
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
. We notice that the phase of the oscillatory integrand
\mathcal{B}_{2,2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}
is non-zero, except when
\sigma_{3}\sigma_{4}=-
. Hence

\mathcal{B}_{2,2,\sigma_{3},-\sigma_{3}}(t,k)=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\Omega^{(1)}_{\sigma_{3},-\sigma_{3}}(k)}\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,k-\ell)\wedge[\ell\wedge\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell)]+\text{oscillatory terms},

where
\Omega^{(1)}_{\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(k)
is defined in (
4.34
).

We conclude that

\displaystyle-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{4},\phi\right\rangle=-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{k\in\boldsymbol{1}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}k\cdot\left(d_{0,-}(t)\wedge\left[k\wedge\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,k)\left(1+|k|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right]\right)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}k\cdot\left(\sum_{\ell\in\boldsymbol{1}}\widehat{d_{1,-}}(t,k-\ell)\wedge\left[\ell\wedge\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right]\right)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{\sigma\in\{\pm\}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}k\cdot\left(\sum_{\ell\in\Omega^{(1)}_{\sigma,-\sigma}(k)}\left(1+|k-\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{d_{2,-\sigma}}(t,k-\ell)\wedge[\ell\wedge\widehat{d_{2,\sigma}}(t,\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}]\right)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt.

(4.62)

The study of the fourth term in (
4.47
), which corresponds to the remainder, follows by using the Sobolev version of inequality (
2.6
) in Lemma
2.2
and is converging to zero.

Conclusion
: Finally, by collecting (
4.45
), (
4.53
), (
4.54
), (
4.56
), (
4.60
), and (
4.62
) we get the equation satisfied by the corrector
d_{1,+}
given by (
4.33
).

Equation for the solenoidal correctors
:
We now derive the equation for the solenoidal correctors by looking at (
3.19
). We proceed similarly to the previous case starting from the l.h.s. of (
4.31
): Given
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}((0,T)\times\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x})
and by taking the adjoints of the respective operators, we get

\displaystyle\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{tt}^{2}\nabla_{x}\wedge E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}}

\displaystyle+(1-\Delta_{x})\nabla_{x}\wedge E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}},\phi\rangle=\left\langle\nabla_{x}\wedge E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}},\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{tt}^{2}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}\phi+(1-\Delta_{x})T_{2,-}^{\varepsilon}\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}},\nabla_{x}\wedge\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{tt}^{2}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}\phi+(1-\Delta_{x})T_{2,-}^{\varepsilon}\phi\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}},\nabla_{x}\wedge\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{tt}^{2}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}\phi+(1-\Delta_{x})T_{2,-}^{\varepsilon}\phi\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\varepsilon T_{2,+}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}},\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\wedge\left(\varepsilon^{2}T_{2,+}^{\varepsilon}\partial_{tt}^{2}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}\phi+(1-\Delta_{x})\phi\right)\right\rangle,

since the curl operator and
(1-\Delta_{x})
commute with
T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\pm}
.
By part (
1
) of Proposition
4.4
, we know that
\varepsilon T_{2,+}^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}}\rightharpoonup d_{2,+}
in
L^{2}_{t,x}
, moreover

\displaystyle\frac{\hbox{\hskip 57.93057pt\hskip-57.93057pt\hbox{$\varepsilon^{2}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\partial_{tt}^{2}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}\phi+(1-\Delta_{x})\phi$}\hskip-57.93057pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.44016pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 57.93057pt}}{\varepsilon}(t,k)=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\exp\left(-\mathrm{i}\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}t}{\varepsilon}\right)\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{tt}^{2}\left(\exp\left(\mathrm{i}\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}t}{\varepsilon}\right)\widehat{\phi}(t,k)\right)\quad

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(1+|k|^{2})\widehat{\phi}(t,k)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\exp\left(-\mathrm{i}\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}t}{\varepsilon}\right)\left[-\frac{1+|k|^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\widehat{\phi}(t,k)+\frac{2\mathrm{i}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}}{\varepsilon}\partial_{t}\widehat{\phi}(t,k)+\partial_{tt}^{2}\widehat{\phi}(t,k)\right]\exp\left(\mathrm{i}\frac{\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}t}{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(1+|k|^{2})\widehat{\phi}(t,k)

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{i}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}\partial_{t}\widehat{\phi}(t,k)+\varepsilon\partial_{tt}^{2}\widehat{\phi}(t,k).

It follows that

\frac{\hbox{\hskip 57.93057pt\hskip-57.93057pt\hbox{$\varepsilon^{2}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\partial_{tt}^{2}T_{2,-}^{\varepsilon}\phi+(1-\Delta_{x})\phi$}\hskip-57.93057pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.44016pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 57.93057pt}}{\varepsilon}\to 2\mathrm{i}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}\partial_{t}\widehat{\phi}\quad\text{in}\quad H^{1}\quad\text{strongly}.

We conclude that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}

\displaystyle\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{tt}^{2}\nabla_{x}\wedge E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}}+(1-\Delta_{x})\nabla_{x}\wedge E^{\varepsilon}_{\text{sol}},\phi\rangle=\left\langle d_{2,+},\nabla_{x}\wedge\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\left\{2\mathrm{i}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}\partial_{t}\widehat{\phi}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\nabla_{x}\wedge d_{2,+},\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\left\{2\mathrm{i}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}\partial_{t}\widehat{\phi}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\right)\right\rangle=-\left\langle\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\left\{2\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}k\wedge\partial_{t}\widehat{d_{2,+}}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\right),\phi\right\rangle.

(4.63)

Next, we want to analyse the r.h.s. of (
4.31
).
By recalling definitions (
4.46
), we study

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}h^{\varepsilon},\phi\rangle=\lim_{t\to 0}\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(\nabla_{x}\wedge\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}(t,x)-\nabla_{x}\wedge\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(t,x)-\nabla_{x}\wedge\mathcal{B}^{\varepsilon}(t,x)-\nabla_{x}\wedge R^{\varepsilon}(t,x)\right),\phi\rangle,

(4.64)

where
R^{\varepsilon}
is defined in (
3.15
).

Limit of the
\nabla_{x}\wedge\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}
term
: We study the first term on the r.h.s. of (
4.64
), i.e., we consider

\nabla_{x}\wedge\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=\nabla_{x}\wedge(\partial_{x_{i}}\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t,x))+\nabla_{x}\wedge(\partial_{x_{i}}\mathcal{\widetilde{J}}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(t,x)),

(4.65)

where

\mathcal{\widetilde{J}}_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t,x):=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x)(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x))_{i}\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x)\mu(d\Theta)

and

\mathcal{\widetilde{J}}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(t,x):=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x)[v(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x))_{i}v(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x))-(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x))_{i}\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(t,x)]\mu(d\Theta),

for
i\in\{1,2,3\}
. The treatment of
\mathcal{\widetilde{J}}_{2}^{\varepsilon}
is done using Lemma
2.3
on the difference between the relativistic and non relativistic velocity. Therefore,
\mathcal{\widetilde{J}}_{2}^{\varepsilon}
is a remainder term of order
\varepsilon^{2}
which is strongly converging to zero.

By the definition of
w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}=\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}-W^{\varepsilon}
and (
4.2
), we have

\mathcal{\widetilde{J}}_{1}^{\varepsilon}=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}((w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})_{i}+W^{\varepsilon}_{i})(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+W^{\varepsilon})\mu(d\Theta)=:\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}+\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{3}+\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4},

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})_{i}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta),\qquad\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}:=W^{\varepsilon}\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})_{i}\mu(d\Theta),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{3}

\displaystyle:=W^{\varepsilon}_{i}\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta),\qquad\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}:=W^{\varepsilon}_{i}W^{\varepsilon}\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta).

For
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
, since
\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})_{i}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\to\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)
in
L^{2}
strongly by Proposition
4.2
, we obtain, using part (
2
) of Lemma
4.3
,

T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{1}=T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})_{i}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\right)\rightharpoonup 0\quad\text{in}\quad L^{2}_{t,x}.

Concerning
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, we have
\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})_{i}\mu(d\Theta)\to\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}\mu(d\Theta)
in
L^{2}
strongly by Proposition
4.2
. Therefore, as in (
4.48
), we get

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})_{i}\mu(d\Theta)\right)W^{\varepsilon}=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}\mu(d\Theta)\right)W^{\varepsilon}.

Then, by part (
2
) Proposition
4.4
and proceeding as in (
4.49
) we get

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left[\left(\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}\mu(d\Theta)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left.\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{0,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{1,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}(\widetilde{d}_{2,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})\right)\right].

Moreover, by part (
3
) of Lemma
4.3
, we notice that,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left[\left(\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}\mu(d\Theta)\right)\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{0,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{1,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}(\widetilde{d}_{2,-})\right)\right]=0.

Therefore, we conclude that the following identity holds:

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left[\left(\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}\mu(d\Theta)\right)\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})\right)\right].

(4.66)

Taking
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}((0,T)\times\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x})
and defining
\psi(t,x):=\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}\mu(d\Theta)\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
, we compute the weak limit of the first term in (
4.66
). By using the expression for
T_{2,+}^{\varepsilon}
,
T_{1,-}^{\varepsilon}
and the one of
\widetilde{d}_{0,+}
given by (
4.21
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(\psi T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})\right),\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp\left(\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\varepsilon}\left(1-\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}\right)t\right)\widehat{\psi}(t,k)d_{0,+}(t)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt,

where we used that
d_{0,+}
is spatially homogeneous.
Therefore, the last integral is always of oscillatory type except when
k=0
. However, recalling expression (
4.65
), we note that we still have to take a derivative
\partial_{x_{i}}
for
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
. Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\partial_{x_{i}}\left(\psi T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}\widetilde{d}_{0,+}\right),\phi\right\rangle=0.

Then, we compute the weak limit of the second term in (
4.66
). By using Plancherel identity, the expression for
T_{2,+}^{\varepsilon}
,
T_{1,-}^{\varepsilon}
and the one of
\widetilde{d}_{1,+}
given by (
4.21
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(\psi T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})\right),\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad=-\mathrm{i}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}t}{\varepsilon}\right)\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\widehat{\psi}(t,k-\ell)\exp\left(\mathrm{i}\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)\widehat{d_{1,+}}(t,\ell)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt

\displaystyle\qquad=-\mathrm{i}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\exp\left(\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\varepsilon}\left(1-\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}\right)t\right)\widehat{\psi}(t,k-\ell)\widehat{d_{1,+}}(t,\ell)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt.

Therefore, the last integral is always of oscillatory type except when
k=0
. However, recalling expression (
4.65
), we note that we still have to take a derivative
\partial_{x_{i}}
for
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
. Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\partial_{x_{i}}\left(\psi T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}\widetilde{d}_{1,+}\right),\phi\right\rangle=0.

We now look at the third term in (
4.66
), that is,

\displaystyle\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(\psi T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})\right),\phi\right\rangle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp{\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}t}{\varepsilon}\right)}

\displaystyle\quad\times\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\widehat{\psi}(t,k-\ell)\exp{\left(\frac{\mathrm{i}\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}t}{\varepsilon}\right)}\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\left(1+\left\lvert\ell\right\rvert^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\exp\left(\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\varepsilon}\left(\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}-\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}\right)t\right)\widehat{\psi}(t,k-\ell)\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\left(1+\left\lvert\ell\right\rvert^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt

(4.67)

Therefore, the last integral is always of oscillatory type except when
\left\lvert\ell\right\rvert=\left\lvert k\right\rvert
.

We conclude that the only contribution for
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
is given by (
4.67
). Hence

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}

\displaystyle\left\langle T_{2,+}^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\wedge(\partial_{x_{i}}\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}),\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\\
|\ell|=|k|\end{subarray}}k\wedge\left(k_{i}\int_{M}\hbox{\hskip 18.49165pt\hskip-18.49165pt\hbox{$\rho_{\Theta}(w_{\Theta})_{i}$}\hskip-18.49165pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.80013pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 18.49165pt}(t,k-\ell)\mu(d\Theta)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{d}_{2,+}(t,\ell)\right)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt.

(4.68)

By noticing the symmetry between the definitions of
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{3}
, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}

\displaystyle\left\langle T_{2,+}^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\wedge(\partial_{x_{i}}\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{3}),\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\\
|\ell|=|k|\end{subarray}}k\wedge\left(k_{i}\int_{M}\hbox{\hskip 12.93811pt\hskip-12.93811pt\hbox{$\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}$}\hskip-12.93811pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{15.60567pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 12.93811pt}(t,k-\ell)\mu(d\Theta)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\widehat{d}_{2,+})_{i}(t,\ell)\right)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt.

(4.69)

Finally, we study
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}
.
We know that
\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\to 1
in
L^{2}
strongly by Proposition
4.2
. Therefore, by adding and subtracting the limit as in (
4.48
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}W^{\varepsilon}_{i}W^{\varepsilon}\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}W^{\varepsilon}_{i}W^{\varepsilon}.

By part (
2
) of Proposition
4.4
and proceeding as in (
4.49
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(W^{\varepsilon}_{i}W^{\varepsilon}\right)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\{0,1,2\},\\
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3}}(\widetilde{d}_{\sigma_{1},-\sigma_{3}})_{i}T^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{2},\sigma_{4}}(\widetilde{d}_{\sigma_{2},-\sigma_{4}})\right).

where we defined
T_{0,\pm}^{\varepsilon}=T_{1,\pm}^{\varepsilon}
for notational convenience.
We now study, for
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\{0,1,2\}
,
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
and
\psi_{1}:=(\widetilde{d}_{\sigma_{1},-\sigma_{3}})_{i},\psi_{2}:=\widetilde{d}_{\sigma_{2},-\sigma_{4}}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
, which terms in

\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k):=\mathcal{F}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3}}(\psi_{1})T^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{2},\sigma_{4}}(\psi_{2})\right)\right)(t,k)

give a non vanishing contribution as
\varepsilon
goes to zero.

If
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\{0,1\}
, using the formula for
T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}
and
T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\pm}
given in (
4.20
), we have

\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k)=\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\left[\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}+\sigma_{3}1+\sigma_{4}1\right]\right)\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,k-\ell)\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell)

with
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
. We notice that, for
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\{0,1\}
, the phase of the oscillatory integrand
\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}
is non-zero, except when
\sigma_{3}=\sigma_{4}=-
, in this case the phase is zero for
|k|=\sqrt{3}
, i.e.,

\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},-,-}(t,k)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,k-\ell)\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell)+\text{oscillatory terms}

for
k\in\boldsymbol{1}=\{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}:\ell_{i}\in\{\pm 1\},i\in\{1,2,3\}\}
.

We observe that for
\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{2}=0
,

\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}_{0,0,-,-}(t,k)

gives a zero contribution in the limit, since
\widetilde{d}_{0,\pm}(t)
are spatially independent.
Next, for
\sigma_{1}=0
and
\sigma_{2}=1
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}_{0,1,-,-}(t,k)

\displaystyle=(\widetilde{d}_{0,+}(t))_{i}\widehat{\widetilde{d}_{1,+}}(t,k)+\text{oscillatory terms}

for
k\in\boldsymbol{1}=\{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}:\ell_{i}\in\{\pm 1\},i\in\{1,2,3\}\}.
By symmetry, the same formula holds for
\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}_{1,0,-,-}(t,k)
.

If
\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{2}=2
we have

\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}_{2,2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\left[\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}+\sigma_{3}\sqrt{1+|k-\ell|^{2}}+\sigma_{4}\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}\right]\right)\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,k-\ell)\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell)

with
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
. We observe that the phase of the oscillatory integrand
\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}_{2,2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}
is always non-zero.

If
\sigma_{1}=2
and
\sigma_{2}=0
, we have

\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}_{2,0,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}[\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}+\sigma_{3}\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}+\sigma_{4}1]\right)\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,\ell)\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,k-\ell).

We notice that the phase of the oscillatory integrand
\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}_{2,0,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}
can be zero, if
\sigma_{3}=-
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}_{2,0,-,\sigma_{4}}(t,k)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\Omega^{(2)}_{\sigma_{4},-\sigma_{4}}(k)}(\widehat{\widetilde{d}_{2,+}})_{i}(t,\ell)\widehat{\widetilde{d}_{0,-\sigma_{4}}}(t,k-\ell)+\text{oscillatory terms},

where
\Omega^{(2)}_{\sigma_{4},-\sigma_{4}}(k)
is defined by (
4.34
). But this implies that
\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}_{2,0,-,\sigma_{4}}(t,k)
gives a zero contribution in the limit because
\widetilde{d}_{0,-\sigma_{4}}
is spatially homogeneous. The same holds for
\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}_{0,2,\sigma_{3},-}(t,k)
.

If
\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}=2
, we can assume w.l.o.g. that
\sigma_{1}=2,\sigma_{2}=1
and we get

\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}_{2,1,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}[\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}+\sigma_{3}\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}+\sigma_{4}1]\right)\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,\ell)\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,k-\ell)

with
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
. We notice that the phase of the oscillatory integrand
\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}_{2,1,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}
can be zero, if
\sigma_{3}=-
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}_{2,1,-,\sigma_{4}}(t,k)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\Omega^{(2)}_{\sigma_{4},-\sigma_{4}}(k)}\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,\ell)\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,k-\ell)+\text{oscillatory terms},

where
\Omega^{(2)}_{\sigma_{4},-\sigma_{4}}(k)
is defined by (
4.34
).

Collecting the limit contributions given by
\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T_{2,+}^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\wedge\partial_{x_{i}}\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4},\phi\right\rangle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{k\in\boldsymbol{1}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}k\wedge\left(k_{i}(d_{0,+})_{i}(t)\widehat{d_{1,+}}(t,k)\right)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{k\in\boldsymbol{1}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}k\wedge\left(k_{i}d_{0,+}(t)(\widehat{d_{1,+}})_{i}(t,k)\right)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{k\in\boldsymbol{1}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}k\wedge\left(k_{i}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}(\widehat{d_{1,+}})_{i}(t,k-\ell)\widehat{d_{1,+}}(t,\ell)\right)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{\sigma\in\{\pm\}}\sigma\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}k\wedge\left(k_{i}\sum_{\ell\in\Omega^{(2)}_{\sigma,-\sigma}(k)}\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\widehat{d_{2,+}})_{i}(t,\ell)\widehat{d_{1,-\sigma}}(t,k-\ell)\right)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{\sigma\in\{\pm\}}\sigma\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}k\wedge\left(k_{i}\sum_{\ell\in\Omega^{(2)}_{\sigma,-\sigma}(k)}\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)(\widehat{d_{1,-\sigma}})_{i}(t,k-\ell)\right)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt.

(4.70)

Limit of the
\nabla_{x}\wedge\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}
term
: We now study the second term in (
4.64
).
By part (
1
) of Proposition
4.4
and proceeding as in (
4.49
), we have for
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}((0,T)\times\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x})

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot(\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon})\right),\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma_{1}\in\{0,1,2\},\\
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3}}({d_{\sigma_{1},-\sigma_{3}}})\nabla_{x}\cdot T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\sigma_{4}}({d_{1,-\sigma_{4}}})\right),\phi\right\rangle,

where we introduced the notation
T^{\varepsilon}_{0,+}:=T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}
. We note that only
T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}({d_{1,\mp}})
appears with the divergence since
\nabla_{x}\cdot T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\pm}({d_{2,\mp}})=\nabla_{x}\cdot T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\pm}({d_{2,\mp}})=0
, as
d_{0,\mp}
are spatially independent and
d_{2,\mp}
are solenoidal.

Next, we study for
\sigma_{1}\in\{0,1,2\},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
and
\psi_{1}:=d_{\sigma_{1},-\sigma_{3}},\psi_{2}:=d_{1,-\sigma_{4}}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
, which terms in

\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\sigma_{1},1,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k)=\mathcal{F}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3}}(\psi_{1})T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\sigma_{4}}(\nabla_{x}\cdot\psi_{2})\right)\right)(t,k)

give a non vanishing contribution as
\varepsilon
goes to zero.

If
\sigma_{1}\in\{0,1\}
, using the formula for
T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}
and
T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}
given in (
4.20
), we have

\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\sigma_{1},1,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k)=\mathrm{i}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}[\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}+\sigma_{3}1+\sigma_{4}1]\right)\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,k-\ell)\ell\cdot\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell)

with
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
. We notice that, for
\sigma_{1}\in\{0,1\}
, the phase of the oscillatory integrand
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\sigma_{1},1,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}
is non-zero, except when
\sigma_{3}=\sigma_{4}=-
so that the phase is zero for
|k|=\sqrt{3}
, i.e.,

\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\sigma_{1},1,-,-}(t,k)=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,k-\ell)\ell\cdot\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell)+\text{oscillatory terms},

for
k\in\boldsymbol{1}=\{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}:\ell_{i}\in\{\pm 1\},i\in\{1,2,3\}\}
.

If
\sigma_{1}=2
we have

\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,1,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k)=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}[\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}+\sigma_{3}\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}+\sigma_{4}1]\right)\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,\ell)(k-\ell)\cdot\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,k-\ell)

with
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
. We notice that the phase of the oscillatory integrand
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,1,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}
can be zero if
\sigma_{3}=-
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,1,-,\sigma_{4}}(t,k)=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\Omega^{(2)}_{\sigma_{4},-\sigma_{4}}(k)}\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,\ell)(k-\ell)\cdot\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,k-\ell)+\text{oscillatory terms},

where
\Omega^{(2)}_{\eta_{1},\eta_{2}}(k)
is defined by (
4.34
).
Therefore,

\displaystyle-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\nabla_{x}\wedge\left(\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot(\varepsilon E_{\text{irr}}^{\varepsilon})\right),\phi\right\rangle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{k\in\boldsymbol{1}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}k\wedge\left(d_{0,+}(t)k\cdot\widehat{d_{1,+}}(t,k)\right)\,\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{k\in\boldsymbol{1}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}k\wedge\left(\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\widehat{d_{1,+}}(t,k-\ell)\ell\cdot\widehat{d_{1,+}}(t,\ell)\right)\,\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{\sigma\in\{\pm\}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}k\wedge\left(\sum_{\ell\in\Omega^{(2)}_{\sigma,-\sigma}(k)}\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)(k-\ell)\cdot\widehat{d_{1,-\sigma}}(t,k-\ell)\right)\,\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt.

(4.71)

Limit of the
\nabla_{x}\wedge\mathcal{B}^{\varepsilon}
term
: We now study the third term in (
4.64
), i.e., we consider

\nabla_{x}\wedge\mathcal{B}^{\varepsilon}=\nabla_{x}\wedge\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\varepsilon}+\nabla_{x}\wedge\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\varepsilon},

where

\mathcal{B}^{\varepsilon}_{1}:=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge B^{\varepsilon}

and

\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(t,x):=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}[v(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})-\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}]\mu(d\Theta)\wedge B^{\varepsilon}.

The treatment of
\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\varepsilon}
is done using Lemma
2.3
on the difference between the relativistic and non relativistic velocity. Therefore,
\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\varepsilon}
is a remainder term of order
\varepsilon^{2}
which is strongly converging to zero.
Therefore, we only focus on the
\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\varepsilon}
term. By recalling the two formulas in (
4.17
), we have

\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\varepsilon}=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}(w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}+W^{\varepsilon})\mu(d\Theta)\wedge\left(b^{\varepsilon}(t,x)-\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon}\right)=:\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}+\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2}+\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{3}+\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{4},

where

\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}:=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge b^{\varepsilon},\quad\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2}:=\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)W^{\varepsilon}\wedge b^{\varepsilon},

\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{3}:=-\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon}),\quad\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{4}:=-\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)W^{\varepsilon}\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon}).

For
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
, since
\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge b^{\varepsilon}\to\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge B
in
L^{2}
strongly by Proposition
4.2
, we obtain, using part (
2
) of Lemma
4.3
,

T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}=T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge b^{\varepsilon}\right)\rightharpoonup 0\quad\text{in}\quad L^{2}_{t,x}.

Concerning
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, we know
\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\to 1
and
b^{\varepsilon}\to B
in
L^{2}
strongly by Proposition
4.2
. Therefore, by adding and subtracting these two limits to
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2}=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)W^{\varepsilon}\wedge b^{\varepsilon}\right)=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(W^{\varepsilon}\wedge B\right).

By part (
2
) of Proposition
4.4
and similarly as in (
4.49
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2}=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{0,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{1,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}(\widetilde{d}_{2,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})\right)\wedge B\right).

However, by part (
3
) of Lemma
4.3
,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left[\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{0,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{1,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}(\widetilde{d}_{2,-})\right)\wedge B\right]=0.

We conclude that the following identity holds:

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2}=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left[\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})\right)\wedge B\right].

(4.72)

Taking
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}((0,T)\times\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x})
, we compute the weak limit of the first term in (
4.72
). By using Plancherel identity, the expressions for
T_{1,\pm}^{\varepsilon}
and
T_{2,+}^{\varepsilon}
given by (
4.20
) and the one of
\widetilde{d}_{0,+}
given by (
4.21
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})\wedge B\right),\phi\right\rangle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\left(\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}-1\right)\right)d_{0,+}(t)\wedge\widehat{B}(t,k)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt.

Hence, the last integral is always of oscillatory type except when
k=0
, and since we take the curl this term vanishes. That is,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\nabla_{x}\wedge\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})\wedge B\right),\phi\right\rangle=0.

Then, we compute the weak limit of the second term in (
4.72
). By using Plancherel identity, the expressions for
T_{1,-}^{\varepsilon}
and
T_{2,+}^{\varepsilon}
given by (
4.20
) and the one of
\widetilde{d}_{1,+}
given by (
4.21
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})\wedge B\right),\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad=-\mathrm{i}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\left(\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}-1\right)\right)\widehat{d_{1,+}}(t,\ell)\wedge\widehat{B}(t,k-\ell)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt.

Hence, the last integral is always of oscillatory type except when
k=0
, and since we take the curl this term vanishes. That is,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\nabla_{x}\wedge\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})\wedge B\right),\phi\right\rangle=0.

We now look at the weak limit of the third term in (
4.72
), i.e.,

\displaystyle\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})\wedge B\right),\phi\right\rangle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp{\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}t}{\varepsilon}\right)}

\displaystyle\quad\times\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp{\left(\frac{\mathrm{i}\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}t}{\varepsilon}\right)}\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\left(1+\left\lvert\ell\right\rvert^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\wedge\widehat{B}(t,k-\ell)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{k,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\exp{\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}(\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}-\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}})\right)}\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\left(1+\left\lvert\ell\right\rvert^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\wedge\widehat{B}(t,k-\ell)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt.

(4.73)

Therefore, the last integral is always of oscillatory type except when
\left\lvert\ell\right\rvert=\left\lvert k\right\rvert
.
We conclude that the only contribution for
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
is given by (
4.73
). Hence

\displaystyle-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}

\displaystyle\left\langle T_{2,+}^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\wedge\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2},\phi\right\rangle=-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\\
|\ell|=|k|\end{subarray}}k\wedge\left(\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\left(1+\left\lvert\ell\right\rvert^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\wedge\widehat{B}(t,k-\ell)\right)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt.

(4.74)

Next, we study
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{3}
. Since
\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}w^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\to\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)
, by adding and subtracting the limit, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{3}=-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon})\right).

Next, by part (
2
) of Proposition
4.4
and similarly as in (
4.49
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{3}

\displaystyle=-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\wedge\left(\nabla_{x}\wedge\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{0,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{1,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}(\widetilde{d}_{2,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\widetilde{d}_{2,+})\right)\right)\right).

Observe that
\nabla_{x}\wedge\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{0,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{0,+})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}(\widetilde{d}_{1,-})+T^{\varepsilon}_{1,-}(\widetilde{d}_{1,+})\right)=0
since
\widetilde{d}_{0,\pm}
are spatially independent and
\widetilde{d}_{1,\pm}
are irrotational. Moreover, by by part (
3
) of Lemma
4.3
and by commuting the curl with
T_{2,+}^{\varepsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\wedge\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}(\nabla_{x}\wedge\widetilde{d}_{2,-})\right)\right)=0.

Hence, the following identity holds:

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{3}=-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left[\left(\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\right)\wedge T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\nabla_{x}\wedge\widetilde{d}_{2,+})\right].

Taking
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}((0,T)\times\mathbb{T}^{3}_{x})
and
\psi(t,x):=\int_{M}\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
, we compute the weak limit of the last equation. By using Plancherel identity, the expression (
4.20
) for
T_{2,\pm}^{\varepsilon}
and the one of
\widetilde{d}_{2,+}
given by (
4.21
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(\psi\wedge T^{\varepsilon}_{2,-}(\nabla_{x}\wedge\widetilde{d}_{2,+})\right),\phi\right\rangle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}t}{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\times\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\widehat{\psi}(t,k-\ell)\wedge\left[\ell\wedge\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right]\exp\left(\mathrm{i}\frac{\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}t}{\varepsilon}\right)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\exp\left(\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\varepsilon}\left(\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}-\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}\right)t\right)\widehat{\psi}(t,k-\ell)\wedge\left[\ell\wedge\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right]\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt.

Hence, the limit is vanishing except when
|\ell|=|k|
, and this gives

\displaystyle-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(\nabla_{x}\wedge\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{3}\right),\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\\
|\ell|=|k|\end{subarray}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}k\wedge\Biggl(\Bigl(\int_{M}\widehat{\rho_{\Theta}w_{\Theta}}(t,k-\ell)d\mu(\Theta)\Bigr)\wedge\left[\ell\wedge\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right]\Biggr)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt.

(4.75)

We finally study
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{4}
. We know that
\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)\to 1
in
L^{2}
strongly by Proposition
4.2
. Therefore, by adding and subtracting the limit as in (
4.48
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{4}=-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(\int_{M}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\mu(d\Theta)W^{\varepsilon}\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon})\right)=-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(W^{\varepsilon}\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon})\right).

By part (
2
) of Proposition
4.4
and proceeding as in (
4.49
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(W^{\varepsilon}\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge W^{\varepsilon})\right),\phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma_{1}\in\{0,1,2\},\\
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3}}(\widetilde{d}_{\sigma_{1},-\sigma_{3}})\wedge(\nabla_{x}\wedge T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\sigma_{4}}(\widetilde{d}_{2,-\sigma_{4}}))\right),\phi\right\rangle,

where we introduced the notation
T^{\varepsilon}_{0,+}:=T_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}
.
We note that only
T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\pm}({\widetilde{d}_{2,\mp}})
appears with the curl operator since
\nabla_{x}\wedge T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}({\widetilde{d}_{0,\mp}})=\nabla_{x}\wedge T^{\varepsilon}_{1,\pm}({\widetilde{d}_{1,\mp}})=0
, as
\widetilde{d}_{0,\mp}
are spatially independent and
\widetilde{d}_{1,\mp}
are irrotational.

We study, for
\sigma_{1}\in\{0,1,2\},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
and
\psi_{1}:=\widetilde{d}_{\sigma_{1},-\sigma_{3}},\psi_{2}:=\widetilde{d}_{2,-\sigma_{4}}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}
, which terms in

\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\sigma_{1},2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k)=\mathcal{F}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{2,+}\left(T^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3}}(\psi_{1})\wedge T^{\varepsilon}_{2,\sigma_{4}}(\nabla_{x}\wedge\psi_{2})\right)\right)

give a non vanishing contribution as
\varepsilon
goes to zero.
If
\sigma_{1}\in\{0,1\}
we have

\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\sigma_{1},2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k)=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}[\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}+\sigma_{3}1+\sigma_{4}\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}]\right)\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,k-\ell)\wedge[\ell\wedge\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell)]

with
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
. We notice that, for
\sigma_{1}\in\{0,1\}
, the phase of the oscillatory integrand
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\sigma_{1},2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}
is non-zero, except when
\sigma_{4}=-
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\sigma_{1},2,\sigma_{3},-}(t,k)=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\Omega^{(2)}_{\sigma_{3},-\sigma_{3}}(k)}\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,k-\ell)\wedge[\ell\wedge\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell)]+\text{oscillatory terms},

where
\Omega^{(2)}_{\eta_{1},\eta_{2}}(k)
is defined by (
4.34
). Observe for
\sigma_{1}=0
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{0,2,\sigma_{3},-}
gives a zero contribution.
If
\sigma_{1}=2
we have

\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{2,2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}(t,k)=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathrm{i}t}{\varepsilon}\left[\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}+\sigma_{3}\sqrt{1+|k-\ell|^{2}}+\sigma_{4}\sqrt{1+|\ell|^{2}}\right]\right)\widehat{\psi_{1}}(t,k-\ell)\wedge[\ell\wedge\widehat{\psi_{2}}(t,\ell)]

with
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\{\pm\}
. We observe that the phase of the oscillatory integrand
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{2,2,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}}
is always non-zero.
We conclude that

\displaystyle-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\langle T_{2,+}^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\wedge\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{4},\phi\right\rangle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\sum_{\sigma\in\{\pm\}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\sigma\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}k\wedge\left(\sum_{\ell\in\Omega^{(2)}_{\sigma,-\sigma}(k)}\widehat{d_{1,-\sigma}}(t,k-\ell)\wedge\left[\ell\wedge\widehat{d_{2,+}}(t,\ell)\left(1+|\ell|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right]\right)\overline{\widehat{\phi}}(t,k)dt.

(4.76)

The study of the fourth term in (
4.64
), which corresponds to the remainder,
follows by using the Sobolev version of inequality (
2.6
) in Lemma
2.2
and is converging to zero.

Conclusion
: Finally, by collecting (
4.63
), (
4.68
), (
4.69
), (
4.70
), (
4.71
), (
4.74
), (
4.75
) and (
4.76
) we get the equation satisfied by the corrector
d_{2,+}
given by (
4.5
).
∎

Appendix A
Proofs of Lemma
2.2
and Lemma
2.3

Proof of Lemma
2.2
.

We refer to Lemma 5.6 in
[
21
]
for a proof of (
2.5
) where the following inequality is proved:

\displaystyle\left\lvert v(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})\right\rvert_{\delta}\leq C\left\lvert\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\right\rvert_{\delta},

(A.1)

which is useful for our proof.
We now show (
2.6
). For this, we remind that
\lambda(\xi):=\nabla_{\xi}(v(\xi)-\xi)
for
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
and we explicitly compute this derivative in
\xi
(recall that
\nabla_{\xi}
is a vector gradient):

\displaystyle\lambda(\xi)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\xi}\left(\frac{\xi}{\sqrt{1+\varepsilon^{2}\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{2}}}-\xi\right)=\text{\rm Id}_{3\times 3}\left(\left(1+\varepsilon^{2}\left\lvert\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\right\rvert^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}-1\right)+\xi\otimes\nabla_{\xi}\left(1+\varepsilon^{2}\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\text{\rm Id}_{3\times 3}\left(\left(1+\varepsilon^{2}\left\lvert\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\right\rvert^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}-1\right)-\varepsilon^{2}\frac{\xi\otimes\xi}{\left(1+\varepsilon^{2}\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{2}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}},

where
(\xi\otimes\xi)_{i,j}=\xi_{i}\xi_{j}
for
i,j\in\{1,2,3\}
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\lambda(\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta})=\text{\rm Id}_{3\times 3}\left(\left(1+\varepsilon^{2}\left\lvert\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\right\rvert^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}-1\right)-\varepsilon^{2}\frac{\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\otimes\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}}{\left(1+\varepsilon^{2}\left\lvert\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\right\rvert^{2}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}}.

(A.2)

We estimate both parts separately, for the first term, by the Taylor series

(1+x)^{-\frac{1}{2}}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}(2n)!}{4^{n}(n!)^{2}}x^{n},

(A.3)

we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\text{\rm Id}_{3\times 3}\left(\left(1+\varepsilon^{2}\left\lvert\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\right\rvert^{2}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}-1\right)\right\rvert_{\delta}

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}(2n)!}{4^{n}(n!)^{2}}\varepsilon^{2n}\left\lvert\xi^{\varepsilon}_{\Theta}\right\rvert^{2n}\right\rvert_{\delta}
